Cohen , Nepean &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;To mask or not to mask&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Who should be wearing masks for COVID-19 protection ? Those who are infected or potentially infected should wear masks to prevent viral-laden droplets from spreading to others . Healthy people wearing masks may have these droplets accumulate on the outside of their masks , only to contaminate their hands when removing or touching the masks . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; For years , my own doctor&amp;rsquo ; s office has had masks for visiting patients to don if they had a cough or suspected influenza infection . That&amp;rsquo ; s a sensible precaution to reduce all respiratory infections and have masks used responsibly when there are shortages . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Peter Neumann , Manotick &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Thanks to the Citizen for **28;62909;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; During this time of change and interruption , any degree of normalcy is welcome . &amp;nbsp ; To me that is the dependable , daily arrival of my newspaper . Our medical professionals and health authorities are showing the wealth of competence Canada is fortunate to have . What is evident , of course , is that all of us were woefully unprepared for the current crisis . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Words and wishes of support are comforting , but what the public really needs is thoughtful and measured actions , which remain the badge of real leadership during a crisis . I applaud and thank all those who are unselfishly focused on improving our governance , health , safety and security during this challenging period and who are putting their personal and family imperatives aside for the benefit of the general population . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Being a cancer survivor and having had the honour to work in the 24/7 Customs and Border Services organization once upon a time in my professional life , I tend to empathize with first responders in the medical , safety and security worlds . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I hope that while we are planning for financial assistance to important business concerns and the strata of our society in need , some thought will be given to providing more direct support and a financial reward , recognition award or even a bonus to health and safety front-line workers ( now or later ) in order to thank those who have put their lives on the line everyday . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I will sleep better if some of my tax dollars go to reward our unspoken heroes during the war against the virus . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Thank you to all front-line workers , be they persons disinfecting public places ; those who are leading the battle in hospital corridors ; at ports of entry , airports , disease-control laboratories ; or pharmacies and food suppliers . " data-portal-copyright= " DAVE CHAN " **28;63919;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1584253 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Listen to government , do n't blame it&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In response to those who are blaming the &amp;ldquo ; inaction of the government&amp;rdquo ; for some of the issues arising from the pandemic : &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I have friends who were planning and ready to go on a cruise trip . &amp;nbsp ; Thank God for the wisdom of the travel agent who informed them of cancelling a few hours before they boarded . If they had left and got stuck on the cruise ship , they would expect the government to bring them back and pay for their decision . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Friends , family members , citizens and all those who listen , please take responsibility for your actions . Listen to the instructions of authorities . If you don&amp;rsquo ; t , blame no one but yourself . God bless John F. Kennedy with his famous words : &amp;ldquo ; Ask not what your country can do for you but what you can do for your country . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; We are all in this unfortunate time together and we should help ourselves and each other to conquer this common enemy . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Badih Shadid , Ottawa &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Social distancing is hard in a retirement home&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Retirement homes are great places to spend your older years . I have lived in one since it opened in 2017 . But now comes the coronavirus and how do we cope ? Well , visitors have been barred , which is a good idea since they may have unknowingly come in contact with the bug . But what about the essential people who look after us , the women and men who prepare and serve our food ? The women and men who keep our quarters clean and comfortable ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; These people have families . They go home after their shifts . It is only a matter of time until one of them unwittingly brings the virus into our midst . What then ? I guess a lot of people will get sick , some may die ; &amp;nbsp ; after all , we are in retirement homes . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; We are old and we are told by the medical people that we are more vulnerable because at that age , our immune systems don&amp;rsquo ; t work as well as they once did and many of us have heart , lung , kidney or liver issues . That makes us more susceptible . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Oh , and how about social distancing ? Hard to stay six feet apart when we sit four to a table at meal times . So what should we do ? I guess hunker down and say a little prayer . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Joe Spence , Ottawa &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Do n't downplay the threat , Mr. Denley&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;Re : &lt;a href= " https : **33;63949;TOOLONG ... more damaging , COVID-19 or the panic over it ? &lt;/a&gt; March 17. &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; What rock has Randall Denley been living under ? Because of swab shortages , even people returning from out of the country , and showing signs will be told to self-isolate ; testing will not necessarily be done . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I&amp;rsquo ; m impressed with the steps the government has taken to ensure our financial stability , but people with symptoms need to have the diagnosis confirmed ; only then will we get the true picture . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Nancy Sartori , Gatineau &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;nbsp ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h3 style= " text-align:center ; " &gt;STAY INFORMED : &lt;a href= " http : **34;63984;TOOLONG ... HERE TO SUBSCRIBE TO OUR NEW DAILY COVID-19 NEWSLETTER FOR ALL THE LATEST **28;64020;TOOLONG Ottawa Citizen Editorial BoardFri , 20 Mar 2020 10:39:44 **45;64050;TOOLONG ... letters : COVID-19 -- How we can heal the economic woundshttp : **34;64097;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;64133;TOOLONG ... title= " Even IKEA has closed up shop . Now , how do we tackle financial hardship ? " alt= " Even IKEA has closed up shop . Now , how do we tackle financial hardship ? " data-portal-copyright= " Errol McGihon " **28;64169;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1583136 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Time to rethink the guaranteed basic income&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; As everyone in the world fights a common enemy , it becomes apparent that COVID-19 causes major problems even if one does not have this virus . For many on the margins or who have been laid off , the financial crisis of having no income is very difficult , if not dire . The federal government&amp;rsquo ; s promises of help are all good and well but when will this money flow ? How does one apply for this money ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Some time ago , former senator Hugh Segal advocated for a national basic income program . The reasons for advocating for this were different then but now this program , if it had been developed , would be the saviour for many . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; If and when the world returns to normal ( normal being that people will start fighting each other again , pollution will start belching out of industries , and the rich will start getting their money back on the stock markets ) , maybe our leaders will have the foresight to take a good , hard look at introducing a basic minimum income so all can have a decent quality of life no matter what emergency comes again . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Katharine R. Elliott , Ottawa &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Long-term lessons on preparing the economy&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;div dir= " ltr " &gt; &lt;p&gt;What is being revealed with the COVID-19 restrictions and the empty store shelves is how vulnerable Canada is with regard to food security and goods we count on to be imported . Now it is vital to protect our remaining farmland . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt;Stop expanding cities and instead build land-banks to rent to young farmers . Build processing plants in our country to preserve food while it is in season . Build solar-heated greenhouses on marginal farmland , not single-family housing . At the same time , incentivize manufacturers of the goods and parts we now depend on other countries to provide . As in war-time , we might have to accept lower profits and wages in order to produce these goods for home markets ( NAFTA 2 notwithstanding ) . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt;Instead of a stimulus package to targeted businesses , I wish the government would use those billions of dollars to provide all Canadians with an untaxed , guaranteed living Income and eliminate the need for EI , subsidies and welfare , OAS and disability payments , which keep citizens in a chronic state of poverty and anxiety as jobs disappear . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt;If anything would keep small businesses going , citizens having money to spend on essentials such as food , rent and utilities , and public transit would surely maintain the economy . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt;Carolyn Herbert , Nepean&lt;/p&gt; &lt;/div&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Hotels could also be used as hospitals&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; As COVID-19 spreads , it is inevitable that a large number of people will require hospital admittance so they can get the appropriate medical treatment from experts with the correct equipment . Ontario doesn&amp;rsquo ; t currently have sufficient hospital beds to deal with this emergency . I suggest the provincial government , with backing from federal emergency powers , requisition hotels to serve as temporary hospitals for the duration of the pandemic . Payments can be made to the hotels . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; This would be a win-win situation for both the medical community and the hospitality industry , which&amp;nbsp ; is facing a drastic reduction in business . Hotels are well-positioned to meet this need . They have rooms with enclosed spaces , TVs and telephones to relieve boredom and maintain communications , and reception areas with easy vehicular access . Rooms have windows that in many cases can be opened to allow fresh air . Large beds can accommodate couples who are both COVID-19 positive . Washrooms in room , coffee machines and refrigerators &amp;ndash ; the list goes on . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Governments should do this very soon , before it is too late to use these valuable resources . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Patrick Hampson , Stittsville &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Another health scourge we must confront&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Thanks for your very informative news coverage of this virus . As of March 13 , Canada had had one death and 224 confirmed cases . It seems by comparison to many other jurisdictions , our governments are doing the right things , especially by letting health care professionals take the lead and by following their advice . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Now , compare that to the numbers reported by Elizabeth Payne on March 14 . She reports that in 2018 , 1,922 people across Canada died from another scourge , and that there were also 10,000 to 15,000 confirmed cases . In the same year , Ottawa alone had 24 deaths and 3,500 confirmed cases . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; No , not another virus or bacterium , but a public health issue of great magnitude : road deaths and injuries . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; If only we and our governments kept that in perspective and took equally bold and decisive actions to prevent those fatalities and life-altering injuries , we&amp;rsquo ; d be so much better off . To all readers , please keep in mind that you are more likely to die on the roads than from COVID-19. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; David Welch , Ottawa &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Thanks to these kind Walmart customers&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Last Saturday I went to Walmart for a few items but was unable to get milk as the shelves were completely empty . When I was standing in line at the cash to pay for my purchases , a couple behind me asked if I had found everything on my list . I answered that I had missed out on milk and Kleenex but would come back the next day and the day after if necessary . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The woman immediately handed me a litre of milk and said : &amp;rdquo ; We have three , one-litre cartons . Now my husband and I have a carton each and so do you and the three of us will have milk today . &amp;rdquo ; She also helped find some small packets of Kleenex as there were no more regular boxes of tissues . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It just made my day . I want to thank them as I have no idea who they are . I also think that with people being kind to one another as this couple was with me , we&amp;rsquo ; ll all make it through this crisis . Keep well , everyone . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Madeleine Rivest , Ottawa &lt;/p&gt; &lt;div dir= " ltr " &gt;&lt;strong&gt;What happens on Day 15 ? **27;64199;TOOLONG &lt;div dir= " ltr " &gt;&lt;/div&gt; &lt;div dir= " ltr " &gt; " Fourteen days " seem to be the mantra and it is understood , but why not say what happens after 14 days ? &lt;/div&gt; &lt;div dir= " ltr " &gt;&lt;/div&gt; &lt;div dir= " ltr " &gt;Can people return to normal activities , go to work , or do what they were doing before ? I guess I&amp;rsquo ; m a people person ! &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I have always washed my hands when I return home from anywhere &amp;hellip ; my mother taught me that . " data-portal-copyright= " Errol McGihon " **28;64858;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1580877 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;COVID-19 : Shall we look for a silver lining ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There is a lot of harm to many people in so many ways as the result of COVID-19 . It&amp;rsquo ; s a sad and serious situation . &amp;nbsp ; But perhaps there might be a silver lining , some upside : &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;bull ; Having time to rest , decompress and stop all the crazy running around ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;bull ; Savouring things such as time at home with family and getting to know your children even more , and your children learning about you : &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;bull ; Discovering joy in simple things &amp;ndash ; such as a walk in the sun or rain , reading a book , cooking healthy meals , playing cards and board games : &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;bull ; Catching up on the &amp;ldquo ; to do&amp;rdquo ; list at home : &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;bull ; Learning that working from home works and saving all that commuting time and stress : &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;bull ; Starting new things such as writing a book or article , taking up art , playing a musical instrument , and cooking Jamie Oliver super foods : &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;bull ; Counting our blessings and realizing we do not need half the things we think we do ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;bull ; Truly appreciating health care workers , public servants , and social support programs ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;bull ; Getting to see re-runs of sporting events and shows that we missed ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;bull ; Taking nothing for granted and valuing our health more than anything else ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;bull ; Seeing we can come together when facing a common enemy ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;bull ; Having the opportunity to be still and listen to the melting snow . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Finally , may the experience of social distancing bring us closer together when next gathering with others . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Trevor Shaw , Ottawa &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Airports are breeding grounds for germs&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; International airports are great places for microbes to spread . Aside from not being terribly clean places , departure and arrival protocols are not conducive to effective risk management : people standing in queues are not two metres apart ; those plastic trays at security are not wiped down between uses ; and neither are those immigration and customs machines . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Funnelling international arrivals into fewer airports not only creates crowded situations like the one we saw at Chicago&amp;rsquo ; s O&amp;rsquo ; Hare over the weekend , it also increases the risk of transmission to domestic passengers whose itineraries have them transiting through these same airports . &amp;nbsp ; If international arrivals are expected to self-isolate for 14 days , it follows that domestic arrivals at YVR , YYC , YYZ and YUL should also plan on spending two weeks at home . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It would be a good idea for epidemiologists working at Canada&amp;rsquo ; s public health agencies to consider disease amplification at airports as a discrete epidemic , not entirely international and not entirely community . &amp;nbsp ; Who knows how many Canadians left , say , Iran &amp;ldquo ; clean&amp;rdquo ; but had to travel through Kiev on their way home , or who left other parts of the planet but whose itineraries routed them through London , Paris , Amsterdam or Frankfurt ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Mark Wheeler , Nepean &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;This doctor deserves the Nobel&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I am sad that there is hardly any mention in the media of Dr. Li Wenliang , who was the first to try to warn the world of the novel coronavirus . If it weren&amp;rsquo ; t for him , the effect could have been much worse . I recommend him posthumously for the Nobel prize ; he deserves it . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Aviva King , Ottawa &lt;/p&gt; **26;64888;TOOLONG better way to greet **33;64916;TOOLONG &lt;div&gt;&lt;/div&gt; &lt;div&gt;There is a plethora of confusing information about what to do and what not to do in connection with the COVID-19 pandemic outbreak . We are told there is a need to avoid close contact when greeting each other such as shaking hands , hugging and/or kissing . &lt;/div&gt; &lt;div&gt;&lt;/div&gt; &lt;div&gt;In this context , the ancient vedic system of greeting by folding the palms of one 's two hands in a vertical fashion , and saying the Sanskrit word " Namaste , " meaning " Salutation to you " ( in all humility ) comes to mind as a universal approach and has recently been demonstrated by prominent personalities such as Prince Charles . &lt;/div&gt; &lt;div&gt;&lt;/div&gt; &lt;div&gt;In ancient times , this type of greeting in what is now India might have been adopted to minimize the spread of infectious diseases by giving it a sacred slant . Also , it was custom in those days to insist on washing hands and feet prior to commencing any sacred function , probably for the same reason . &lt;/div&gt; &lt;div&gt;&lt;/div&gt; &lt;div&gt;Dr . Kunnath S. Subramanian , Nepean&lt;/div&gt; &lt;div&gt;&lt;/div&gt; **34;64951;TOOLONG spirit , as we showed during the **30;64987;TOOLONG &lt;div&gt;&lt;/div&gt; &lt;div&gt;&lt;span style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt;As one old enough to remember , I see many similarities ( and differences ) in attitudes toward the present health crisis and the outbreak of the Second World War . **25;65019;TOOLONG &lt;div&gt; &lt;p class= " xMsoNormal " &gt;In the days leading up to war being declared , a renowned British scientist , J.B.S. Haldane , predicted that the opening air raid of the war would result in 100,000 deaths , while the Royal Air Force estimated 20,000 daily casualties . These figures prompted plans for 750,000 hospital beds and a stockpile of one million coffins ; more than 30 million gas masks were issued and air raid shelters issued . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p class= " xMsoNormal " &gt;While I recall no signs of panic at that time , a great sense of community spirit did occur ; normally reserved neighbours came together and official directions were closely followed without complaint . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p class= " xMsoNormal " &gt;In regard to hoarding , there was very little , if any , although I was involved in one minor incident . As part of the war precautions , I had been evacuated with my school from the city to the countryside two days before the actual declaration of war , and the lady with whom I was billeted , recognizing that shortages might occur , sent me to her grocer to ask for one pound of Demerara sugar . The grocer informed me that he had sold out . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p class= " xMsoNormal " &gt;Charles Morton , Manotick&lt;/p&gt; &lt;/div&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Weapons show should not go ahead&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;Re : Major defence conferences in Ottawa to proceed despite virus , March 12. &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It is totally irresponsible for the arms show , CANSEC , to proceed . ( On the CANSEC website , the following is claimed for May 27 to 28 : &amp;ldquo ; Featuring more than 12,000 industry , military and government delegates from Canada and around the world , as well as 300 exhibitors and 55 international delegations , CANSEC is North America&amp;rsquo ; s largest tri-service defence event . &amp;rdquo ; This is a place where Canada sells weapons and surveillance equipment to countries around the world . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Cancelling events and closing public facilities is a major factor in containing the virus . &amp;nbsp ; Thus , having delegations come from 55 countries and bringing 12,000 people to mingle in the E-Y centre is the exact recipe for spreading the virus . &amp;nbsp ; &amp;nbsp ; If those in charge insist on putting profits before people to promote the sale of weapons , the government should intervene and shut them down . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Jo Wood , Ottawa &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;And about that hoarding ... &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The frenzy over purchasing toilet paper prompts the Judy Collins song : &amp;ldquo ; Both Sides Now . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; David E. Hopkins Ottawa &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;It 's serious but we 're **28;65046;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Although the coronavirus has been spreading rapidly ( due in part to unrestricted travel ) , attempts to stop or limit it&amp;rsquo ; s spread appear to be having little impact &amp;hellip ; other than causing significant social and potential economic disruption , leading to possible worldwide recession . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; While the world should naturally be prepared to cope with any such pandemic , it&amp;rsquo ; s hard to see why this particular virus requires so much attention , with work stoppages and other social disruptions . &amp;nbsp ; Perhaps governments worldwide ( or in Canada at least ) should take little action other than establishing emergency treatment facilities in strategic population areas to handle any cases that appear to be more serious ( such as those in the older age groups . ) Restrictions on travel and personal interaction appear unmerited. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It&amp;rsquo ; s time to see this latest virus in a more normal light as something that can be managed , similar to other historic epidemics , without putting national economies and people&amp;rsquo ; s long-term livelihoods at risk . It&amp;rsquo ; s time to limit the media hype and excessive government involvement by taking a more controlled and less reactive approach to handling this latest virus . A website with the related information on symptoms , treatment needs and options should be all that is needed . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Bill Williams , &amp;nbsp ; Richmond &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Taiwan shows the world how to act **26;65076;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Containment is possible . &lt;a href= " https : **33;65104;TOOLONG ... has proved it&lt;/a&gt; &amp;ndash ; despite having many travellers to and from China . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; However , the Taiwanese government started immediately . We do not much hear about the extended families in some communities , where the senior parents live with the family . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Spain and Italy are Europe&amp;rsquo ; s hardest-hit areas , where the extended family is dominant and family members care for elders . In Canada , we have many ethnic groups who readily take care of their elders in an extended family home setting . The various levels of government should encourage such practices , which are both family fulfilling and cost-effective . Even more troubling is the dismissal of such concerns by some members of council and the mayor himself . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Apparently , council was told that there were legal reasons for not being given full information . Why did council not challenge such advice ? It had the final authority and responsibility to approve or not to approve the contract . " alt= " Empty shelves where toilet paper supplies used to be at the Independent in Manotick. " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;65615;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1578961 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Serious coronavirus questions need serious answers&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;div dir= " ltr " &gt;We are grateful for the extensive daily briefings from local and provincial medical authorities assuring us that we are prepared for the worst and reinforcing the need for hand-washing and self-isolation. &lt;/div&gt; &lt;div dir= " ltr " &gt;&lt;/div&gt; &lt;div dir= " ltr " &gt;But I have not heard any realistic answers to the following obvious questions : &lt;/div&gt; &lt;div dir= " ltr " &gt;&lt;/div&gt; &lt;div dir= " ltr " &gt;1 ) How many seriously ill patients can we expect in the Ottawa region , a catchment area of around 1,500,000 people ? ( Based on the experience of Northern Italy , with a similar health care system , that number is some 225 to 250 new critically ill patients. ) &lt;/div&gt; &lt;div dir= " ltr " &gt;&lt;/div&gt; &lt;div dir= " ltr " &gt;2 ) Wait times for adult emergency patients to be admitted to an Ottawa hospital are now in excess of 12 hours . This is before the pandemic hits . Where are you planning to accommodate new patients needing intensive care to deal with COVID-19 ? &lt;/div&gt; &lt;div dir= " ltr " &gt;&lt;/div&gt; &lt;div dir= " ltr " &gt;3 ) Does the region , including private vendors and the military , have enough essential equipment -- masks , hand sanitizer , ventilators , BPAP/CPAP units -- to meet the expected demand ? &lt;/div&gt; &lt;div dir= " ltr " &gt;&lt;/div&gt; &lt;div dir= " ltr " &gt;4 ) Does the region have sufficient trained medical staff -- doctors , nurses , technicians , etc. -- to meet the expected demand ? &lt;/div&gt; &lt;p&gt; Until we get serious answers to such serious questions , the reassurances offered by authorities must surely be regarded as just so much feel-good blather. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Gordon Ritchie , Ottawa &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;We might just die of boredom&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; No Ottawa Senators games to watch . Major league baseball , soccer and basketball all cancelled . The ever popular NCAA March Madness basketball tournament is a no-show . Our St Patrick&amp;rsquo ; s Day parade is cancelled , so are all the events at the Canadian Tire Centre and Lac-Leamy. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It appears that just about any form of entertainment is either cancelled or postponed . Just a few days ago , the greatest fear of many people was dying from the COVID-19 virus but now , for many people , the greatest fear is dying of sheer boredom . Anyone up for a rousing game of Solitude ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Les Shinder , Ottawa &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Bravo to politicians for working together&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I have been watching the news coverage of COVID-19 in the United States and Canada . In the U.S. , we see leaders trying to blame each other , and no one taking on the mantle of true leadership . In Canada , we see Premier Doug Ford and Prime Minister Justin Trudeau coming together from near-opposite ends of the political spectrum to work together to address a national crisis . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Thank goodness my father and mother decided to return to Canada just before&amp;nbsp ; I was born . Here , I can be proud of our leaders , not cringe whenever they speak about such an important&amp;nbsp ; life and death&amp;nbsp ; issue . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Bob Ryerson , Manotick &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Give experts time to find a solution&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Are we heading toward a pandemic crisis like the Black Plague of the 14th century , which took the lives of 45 million people ? Perish the thought . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; However , with the progress made in the scientific and medical fields since , there is hope that the global greatest minds in these sciences will come out with the solution in the near future . No need to panic at this stage . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Let us be optimistic and have faith in our scientists and medical professionals . Follow the established advice and recommendations . Be cautious regarding exposure . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Luc Trepanier , Aylmer &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Think hard about what will actually help frail loved ones&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; We are all very concerned about the capacity of over-burdened emergency departments and acute care hospitals to deal with the wave of patients related to COVID-19. &amp;nbsp ; I am writing to all those family members who have loved ones in nursing homes and are facing difficult decisions about their level of care . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Over the past years , three family members have been in nursing homes and we have had to make difficult decisions regarding their level of care . It is inevitable that those in nursing homes deteriorate over time and eventually require end-of-life care . Do we give direction to the nursing home team to send them to the emergency or not ? At what point is this justified by their condition or is it a transfer that just delays the inevitable ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; My sister died last year after a steady physical and mental deterioration . She received excellent care at Starwood but on a couple of occasions it seemed acute care was justified . In the hospital emergency department , she was a hallway patients for close to a day and she had good care . But did it make any difference to her outcome ? Not at all . &amp;nbsp ; In fact , being away from her familiar environment and caring nursing home staff did more harm than good . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I appreciate how difficult it is as a family member to make what is basically a life or death decision . But consider what is best for your loved ones , think long and hard about the pros and cons of having them sent to an overcrowded emergency surrounded by an unfamiliar staff and environment , exposed to whatever pathogens might be floating around . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Many will not share my opinion . However , I believe , especially at this time of a pandemic , that there is an urgency to ensuring the emergency departments and acute care beds are best utilized . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Kathleen Nunn , Carp &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;There are lots of this virus-killer around&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " xs1 " &gt;As a physician , I am being asked about a product that can kill the coronavirus . The good news is that one exists and it is cheap . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " xs1 " &gt;The virus has a lipid ( fatty ) outer layer . Soap breaks right through this layer and makes the virus fall apart . So when you scrub with soap , you are n't just washing the virus off ; you are destroying it . Soap is actually better for killing this virus on&lt;span class= " xApple-converted-space " &gt; &lt;/span&gt;skin than are sanitizers . I know soap sounds boring but I think we need to envision it like the virus killer superhero it actually is. &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " xs1 " &gt;It 's not The Flash though ; you do need to wash hands thoroughly for 20 seconds or so . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " xs1 " &gt;Dr . Ottawa Citizen Editorial BoardFri , 13 Mar 2020 10:48:29 **45;65983;TOOLONG ... letters : On coronavirus and on green spacehttp : **35;66030;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;66067;TOOLONG ... title= " A tourist wearing a protective mask takes a selfie outside the Sagrada Familia in Barcelona on March 11 , 2020. " alt= " A tourist wearing a protective mask takes a selfie outside the Sagrada Familia in Barcelona on March 11 , 2020. " data-portal-copyright= " LLUIS GENE " **28;66103;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1576686 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Look to the roots of coronavirus in future&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; While it is certainly true that many die of the flu , the mortality rate is estimated to be 0.1 per cent . With COVID-19 however , the WHO reports that 3.4 per cent of those known to be infected die . So although the frequency of transmission may be similar , COVID-19 appears to be many times more deadly than seasonal influenza. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Of course , the lethality of COVID-19 may be revised downward over time as many experiencing milder symptoms may not presently be included in the number of known cases , or it may change because the virus mutates . However , when reporting on the &amp;ldquo ; facts , &amp;rdquo ; it is always wise to include all the relevant information instead of just that which makes us feel most comfortable . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It stands to reason that COVID-19 would present a greater threat than the flu because it transferred to humans from another animal species and therefore we have no immunity to it . The future focus must be on creating a global legally binding treaty which bans the poaching and selling of wildlife for food and fake &amp;ldquo ; medicine . &amp;rdquo ; Both SARS and COVID-19 originated in markets of this type , which are driving many species to extinction , and pose a continuing threat to human health worldwide . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Tiina Baumbach , Ottawa &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Pay attention to our green space&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;Re : &lt;a href= " https : **33;66133;TOOLONG ... housing for the aging population , and you 'll fix it for others too&lt;/a&gt; , March 9. &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I agree with much of Daniel Buckles&amp;rsquo ; opinion piece &amp;ndash ; especially his encouragement to all of us to pay attention to Ottawa&amp;rsquo ; s new official plan and a city-sponsored study of R4 zoning to promote apartments and to think about how to promote healthy aging of our population . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I agree that we need more multi-dwelling buildings in the city , but recent development along the Wellington-Richmond Road corridor is an example that discourages me . Virtually none of it is low-rise , little of it promotes affordable housing , much of it has created a concrete corridor and driven out independent businesses , social organizations and the arts with high rent &amp;ndash ; even when some are initially integrated into the new buildings . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I see too little evidence of rooftop gardens , or changes in bylaws to ensure adequate setbacks that protect or create green space and protect natural light that create walkable neighbourhoods and fight pollution . I am not a fan of the policy that permits developers to avoid integrating green space in these development sites by supporting green space elsewhere in the city boundaries . It seems to me that decreasing green space in a neighbourhood while increasing the population density dramatically is a poor policy in an era where we need trees and green space more than ever ( and makes our cities even noisier ) . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Finally , the city has ongoing concerns about participation in its recycling and green bin programs . I believe there is good participation by single residence dwellings but that high rises ( both residential and business ) aren&amp;rsquo ; t yet pulling their weight with regard to green waste . I hope that this gets addressed in future development . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I may be an anomaly , as we made a deliberate decision to settle in Ottawa because it balanced its development with protection of green space while offering many of the amenities found in larger cities . Green space near my dwelling is important to me and I worry that we risk eroding this precious resource with too many high rises that do not address the issues above . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Linda Murphy , Ottawa &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;nbsp ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN THE NEWS&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;66168;TOOLONG ... Ontario teachers and math -- something 's not adding up&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; Ottawa Citizen Editorial BoardThu , 12 Mar 2020 10:50:06 **45;66273;TOOLONG ... letters : Light rail an example of how politics interferes with procurementhttp : **29;66320;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;66351;TOOLONG ... title= " Light rail : It 's no mystery why the procurement flopped . " alt= " Light rail : It 's no mystery why the procurement flopped . " data-portal-copyright= " Jean Levac " **28;66387;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1571317 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Cause of the LRT fiasco was political&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;Re : &lt;a href= " https : **33;66417;TOOLONG ... the slow-rolling disaster of Ottawa 's $9-billion LRT project&lt;/a&gt; , March 7. &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; My long experience as a procurement lawyer in the federal public sector suggests the cause of the LRT fiasco may be rather less complicated than James Bagnall suggests . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; When public sector procurements go wrong , one generally need look no further than the political masters of those tasked with making the buy . Being a curiously sanguine breed , despite oft-repeated experience suggesting caution and prudence might be in order , the political class habitually demand too much , for too little , too quickly . Make no mistake . They design their procurements this way , with scoring heavily weighted in favour of the lowest price . Cosmetic good value sells well at the polls after all . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But this strategy is unwise in sophisticated projects such as LRT . It tends to elicit unrealistic low-ball offers in circumstances so complex that substance and quality simply must be of the highest order . These are generally rather more , than less expensive . In the LRT procurement process , the three suppliers responding to the Request for Proposals together made it clear beyond a doubt that the RTG price , as the conspicuous outlier , should have been a clue that theirs was a proposal that was entirely too good to be true . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Donald Campbell , Ottawa &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;A positive story about community coping&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;Re : &lt;a href= " https : **33;66452;TOOLONG ... I have worked through the early days of HIV , when we didn&amp;rsquo ; t know what we know now , through SARS , through H1N1 and through countless other infectious outbreaks , and through it all I have never seen a health care worker or first responder shy away from providing care to our patients . It doesn&amp;rsquo ; t mean we weren&amp;rsquo ; t afraid , but we quietly shared our fears with each other and we went to work . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; When the roads are too dangerous for you to go out , we go to work . When you are at home celebrating holidays with your families , we go to work . When our resources are cut , and we must do more with less , we go to work . When our salaries are frozen , we go to work . We get punched , kicked , spit at and sworn at , but we go to work . When COVID-19 hits our community , we will go to work . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; So , to those who are stealing or stockpiling masks and hand sanitizer , and to those , even worse , selling them for an outrageous profit , I say : How dare you ? She shows us how the words we use to describe fatalities on the road may lead to an unconscious acceptance of this situation , merely a result of a normal movement of cars . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; She persuades us that these deaths from collisions on the road are preventable and we must address the causes . One of these causes , which she discussed , was speed . It would be one of the tallest structures in Orl&amp;eacute ; ans and would change the character of the community , not to mention create potential problems of noise , flooding , parking , security and more . The community would also lose green space . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The &amp;nbsp ; CECCE has been placing one-page advertising in local papers , including The Citizen , to obtain the community&amp;rsquo ; s support but it has yet to submit its development plan to the city . Throughout the day we were able , with some difficulty , to clear the driveway . But we knew though the real work was yet to come . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; At just after 9 p.m. , a snowplow not only cleared the street but pushed the existing bank back to widen the road . The result was a pile of monumental proportions . As my wife had headed up to bed , I was on my own . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; To my surprise , however , a neighbour was already hard at work . Together , she and I tackled the job . Before long we heard the plow returning to clear the other side of the street . Then the magic happened . " data-portal-copyright= " MUNIR UZ ZAMAN " **28;67977;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1572130 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;We need to support global vaccine alliance&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I am deeply saddened by the novel coronavirus ( COVID-19 ) for two reasons : first , people are sick and some dying in more and more countries ; second , the fear and panic among westerners is drowning out the voices of &lt;a href= " https : //www.gavi.org/ " &gt;Gavi , the Vaccine Alliance&lt;/a&gt; . Gavi is currently campaigning for funds to enable it to continue to immunize children in developing countries and protect them from diphtheria , measles , cholera , polio and so on . Without organizations such as Gavi , many children in poorer countries would have no access to the vaccines that our children do . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The latest number of reported COVID-19 deaths is almost 3,000 . This is truly frightening . Since Gavi was created in 2000 , however , it is estimated to have prevented 13 million deaths by helping to vaccinate more than 760 million children . And it expects to save seven million more lives over the next five years . A number of countries , including Canada , have already developed a possible vaccine for COVID-19 , and drug trials are beginning . I have no doubt that they will be successful . This crisis will end . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Canada has always been a strong supporter of Gavi . We look to our government to step up once again . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Sherry Moran , Ottawa &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Teachers are n't the only workers who are affected&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In your future coverage of the teachers&amp;rsquo ; strikes , please include those who are intrinsic to our children&amp;rsquo ; s education : school bus drivers , walking school bus workers , lunch monitors and school crossing guards . Unlike the teachers , they do not receive strike pay , nor are they paid on snow days , PD days , March Break or Summer Break . They barely subsist on low wages . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The school bus drivers I have spoken to earn less than $24,000 a year , are only paid from their first pick-up of a child , and contend with both heavy traffic and behavioural outbursts from up to 60 children on their bus ( and their parents , when the bus is late ) . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Maureen Cech , Ottawa &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;What licence plates and LRT have in common&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The debacle with the new Ontario licence plates is reminiscent of the fiasco Ottawa is experiencing with its LRT , albeit on a much smaller scale . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; First , there is a tendency for the provincial government to be less than transparent about the problem with the new plates . Second , there appear to have been some flaws in the procurement process that led to the production of defective products . Third , like the LRT , it appears that proper and thorough testing was not done before the new plates were introduced and delivered to the public . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Finally , the Ontario government must be forthcoming about what caused the problem , how it is being fixed and at what cost to the taxpayers . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Dono Bandoro , Ottawa &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Council should treat Chiarelli with a bit more humanity&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I am saddened that Mayor Jim Watson and his crew at Ottawa City Hall have not reached out to Coun. Currently many police in the U.S. and a few in Canada are using Clearview AI to match a picture of a suspect with a huge database of pictures and information gleaned from Facebook , Instagram and other popular Internet media . Very useful , I&amp;rsquo ; m sure . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But what happens when this software gets into the hands of ordinary Canadians , which it undoubtedly will . Imagine that someone with a hate on for the police , or any public figure , takes a photo of a cop and matches it with all that law officer&amp;rsquo ; s Internet data , including address , phone numbers and family members . With that info , it isn&amp;rsquo ; t hard for a determined person to get hold of even more sensitive personal information . A tool for law enforcement will now become a devastating tool against our public servants , and , in fact , all of us. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Another analogy ? The double-edged sword . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ian McMaster , Ottawa &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Taxes and fees are killing our small **25;68327;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;Re : &lt;a href= " https : **33;68354;TOOLONG ... worrisome remake of our traditional main streets , &lt;/a&gt; March 2. &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The main culprit in these closings is the city biting off the hand that feeds it . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ottawa has chewed on small businesses , and the subsequent bleeding is killing them off one at a time . Property taxes , business taxes , parking fees , patio permits and all those hidden fees are difficult , if not impossible , to take if one wants to own a family business . &lt;br&gt; The city&amp;rsquo ; s &amp;ldquo ; we know best&amp;rdquo ; attitude has all but sucked the life out of trying to make a living . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Add to that the federal government eliminating the opportunity to pay family members dividends and deduct the expense ; it has thrown away any nest-egg or safety net that once existed . Small business can absorb minimum wage gains but not the chunks of other items being thrown at them . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The transition will be to restaurants , then vacant spaces below increasingly dense condo-style buildings . " data-portal-copyright= " Wayne Cuddington " **28;68684;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1568741 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;LRT is a toy train not suited to our city&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Some city councillors are talking about getting out of the LRT service contract . Given all the major , varied and repeated failures , can we really expect another system operator to do much better ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In my view , the LRT is a toy train not suited to Ottawa . The fundamental flaw was the initial choice in selecting a &amp;ldquo ; light&amp;rdquo ; rail system driven by electricity . It seems prudent to put LRT expansion on pause and to critically assess whether we have the right system here . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And , in the meantime , we should seriously consider restoring bus service as the principal mode of public transit downtown ; while perhaps keeping the existing LRT as an alternative or supplemental/parallel system . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Our mayor has a big call to make . At what point does he recognize we have systemic failure &amp;ndash ; or does he press on in the hope things get better eventually ? Can you visualize what LRT expansion 2 and 3 will be like , given LRT experience to date ? Have a heart . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Linda Berg , Almonte &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Canada needs its own Mahatma Gandhi&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;Re : &lt;a href= " https : **33;68935;TOOLONG ... lessons learned at Oka about Indigenous protest reverberate today&lt;/a&gt; , Feb. 27. &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I wish a modern-day Mahatma Gandhi could be present in Canada to bring the parties together and help resolve history-based grievances , disputes and possible misunderstandings . Let there be goodwill , peace and safety . Please , let harmony and humanity prevail . Please , let Canada&amp;rsquo ; s reputation as a renowned peace-maker prevail . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The world is watching the worrisome , sad events unfolding here . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ratna Ray , Ottawa &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Who throws out hereditary chiefs if they blunder ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I am amazed that &amp;ldquo ; hereditary chiefs&amp;rdquo ; of Indigenous People have this &amp;ldquo ; inherited &amp;rdquo ; power to act and decide what&amp;rsquo ; s best for their community . This is not how democracy is supposed to work . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Centuries ago , kings and monarchs were hereditary rulers who made laws and ruled over their subjects . This all changed when people took the power away from monarchs and democracy was born . Our own Queen is just a figurehead : she does not make laws that govern Canada . The trouble with undemocratic governing is that it&amp;rsquo ; s open to uncontrollable corruption and abuse of power . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In a nutshell , it is the elected representatives of the people who make laws and speak for the people . If elected officials make mistakes and mess up , the people will throw them out at the next election . But who throws out the &amp;ldquo ; hereditary chiefs&amp;rdquo ; if they blunder or make bad choices ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Most Indigenous bands now have democratically elected leaders . It is they who should speak for their people , not the throwback from undemocratic times , the &amp;ldquo ; hereditary chiefs . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Rafal Pomian , Ottawa &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;68970;TOOLONG ... title= " Highway 6 in Caledonia was blocked in support of the Wet'suwet'en hereditary chiefs in B.C. " alt= " Highway 6 in Caledonia was blocked in support of the Wet'suwet'en hereditary chiefs in B.C. " data-portal-copyright= " GEOFF ROBINS " **28;69006;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1568746 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;All sides in protest must work for ' achievable ' goals&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In many respects , the current protests and rail blockades dotting the country share much with the &amp;ldquo ; Occupy&amp;rdquo ; movement of 2011 . While both express legitimate frustrations and historical grievances , they &lt;br&gt; make/made for a moving target , by rolling up more and more grievances into the same conceptual cloud . &amp;nbsp ; The Occupy movement was not especially successful , and I am not very hopeful that the Wet&amp;rsquo ; suwet&amp;rsquo ; en protests will benefit that nation , either . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Stated plainly , telling people on the east coast that they can not have heating in their homes , or that western workers with no other livelihood are simply out of luck and work until reconciliation is achieved , makes no sense . It is like telling First Nations that they are not going to see any efforts on potable water until climate-change is successfully addressed . Neither approach leads to a happy or &lt;br&gt; timely ending . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; That does not mean that the blockades are pointless or unjustified . Rather , whatever is intended to be achieved needs to be focused , discuss-able , and observably actionable . Identifying a pipeline route that makes for the least environmental threat , and least disruption to Wet&amp;rsquo ; suwet&amp;rsquo ; en traditional ways , in a feasible manner , is a sensible and legitimate objective , and an achievable one . Those who think they are defeating the fossil fuel industry &amp;ldquo ; dragon&amp;rdquo ; by blocking ANY pipeline are living in a Quixotic dream . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The fossil fuel industry certainly needs to wind down , strategically , but can not come to a screeching halt . &lt;br&gt; Provincial and federal governments have to insist that they need a short list of achievables , so that they can sit down , talk , and get something done . It is not enough to simply declare &amp;ldquo ; You&amp;rsquo ; re hurting the Canadian economy . Abandon your grievances and blockades before we can talk . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Mark Hammer , Ottawa &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;An example of leadership from business&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Bravo to CN and CP for putting aside competitive business issues in order to get the freight trains moving again . Now that&amp;rsquo ; s leadership . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Alex Remillard , Ottawa &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Time to make education an essential service&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ontario premiers Bob Rae , Mike Harris , Dalton McGuinty and Kathleen Wynne all succumbed to the blackmail of the teachers&amp;rsquo ; unions . It appears easier to settle excessive demands than deal with the issues . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The unions have achieved this by repeatedly taking job action because it provides them leverage from the public , who are greatly inconvenienced . This blackmailing affects at least three million Ontarians . The teachers have achieved significant concessions including high wages , control over teacher-student ratios , excessive holidays , pay increases for years of service irrespective of merit performance , full pension plans that allow for retirement earlier than most Canadians , and control over substitute-teaching , among others . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It&amp;rsquo ; s about time the Ontario government got tough and advised unions that education is a provincial responsibility and teachers are employees . Employers , not unions , need to set the standards for class size , on-line courses , hours of work and curriculum . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ontario teachers are the highest paid in the country . Ontarians should have expectations that their education system is the best , with student performance and the quality of education being exemplary . This is not the case . When the actions and dictates of a few affect the many and are used as a negotiating ploy , then there is an inherent unfairness in the system . It is time to consider deeming Ontario education an essential service . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Roger Harbord , Prospect , Ont. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Why I am walking with the teachers&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Premier Doug Ford wants the public to believe that the teachers&amp;rsquo ; strikes are about a two-per-cent wage increase for teachers because the government is offering only a one-per-cent increase . Really ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Truth be told , the strikes are all about money , but not money for wages . The issue teachers are striking for is the lack of money to keep classroom sizes as they are , the loss of educational assistants who work with special needs students , and introducing online courses for high school students so they have less time with their teachers . In fact , high school students need more time in this complex and ever-changing world . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Striking teachers have already lost way more than one per cent of their salaries due to strike days , yet they continue to fight for the issues . Today&amp;rsquo ; s teachers have at least two degrees from various universities , and are the best educated teachers we have ever had . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Teachers are out on those picket lines fighting for your children , who need as much time and guidance as possible ; and seeing that special need students get the extra help of educational assistants . Today&amp;rsquo ; s teachers face students with many learning abilities in each class . There can be as many as three or four ability levels in just one classroom . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; As a retired teacher from Toronto , now living in Eastern Ontario , l am walking with these wonderful teachers , who strongly believe that the children of today are our future , and deserve all the opportunities that our education system can provide . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; If the Ford government truly wants to save big money in education , without hurting the students and the quality of their education , it should eliminate the Catholic education system , which doubles educational costs right across the province . Let&amp;rsquo ; s go to one public system and give children every possible opportunity to succeed . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Wendy Hassard , Perth &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Thank you for disagreeing so civilly&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I spent the afternoon last Sunday going door-to-door for a candidate running in the Orl&amp;eacute ; ans provincial byelection . " data-portal-copyright= " DNDCpl Simon Duchesne " **28;69745;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1565475 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; **25;69775;TOOLONG report based on extensive research&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;Re : &lt;a href= " https : **33;69802;TOOLONG ... Forces nixes post about military ' bling ' but moves ahead with other initiatives&lt;/a&gt; , Feb. 14. &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; This article , which is certain to have raised eyebrows , contained a number of references to a study that I authored in 2017 ( the Tiger Team Report ) . Lacking context , it portrays certain slogans as having come from my keyboard ( For instance : &amp;ldquo ; my bling are my medals&amp;rdquo ; ; &amp;ldquo ; my war paint is camouflage&amp;rdquo ; and the catchy &amp;ldquo ; of course I throw like a girl , but I never miss&amp;rdquo ; ) . The reader may be left with the inaccurate impression that these were part of the Tiger Team report , which is wrong . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Two key documents , as well as four working groups , were used to arrive at the recommendations : the Earnscliffe Strategy Group study of 2016 , which held 14 focus groups involving approximately 2,000 women ; and a second report by DGMPRA ( 2017 ) crafted from 45 focus groups held across 12 CAF locations with 335 Regular Force women of all ranks . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; These in-depth studies paved the way for the work that was accomplished by the Tiger Team , and although some of the recommendations may seem questionable , they are backed up by detailed research from both the civilian and military sector ; of women from all age groups , backgrounds and ranks . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; If military personnel took offence , or believed these suggestions were part of the TT report , then this needs to be corrected . To move the yardstick forward , to achieve the government&amp;rsquo ; s target for women in the CAF , everything must be explored . Ideas must be nurtured and developed ; some pursued , others dropped . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; While tangling together details from disparate sources results in an amusing read , I implore the reader to learn the truth and contribute to a positive dialogue . For somewhere , there is a key that will open the door for women to view the CAF as a viable career option . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Suzanne Raby , Thornbury , Ont. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Protests about more than ' inconvenience ' &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;div dir= " ltr " &gt;I have been closely following media coverage of the protester blockades of the rail network in Canada . Then it was the report by economists David Mackie and Jessica Murray at JPMorgan Chase . Now it is these 40 Nobel prize winners . The United Nation&amp;rsquo ; s report from the IPCC in 2018 urged &amp;ldquo ; rapid , far reaching and unprecedented&amp;rdquo ; action to combat the effects on us of climate change . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; When Greta started her protest , many said that she should go back to school and leave the world&amp;rsquo ; s problems to the &amp;ldquo ; experts . &amp;rdquo ; JPMorgan Chase is the largest financial backer of the fossil fuel companies in the world ( the experts ? ) . Its report says that as the effects of climate change continue &amp;ldquo ; we can not rule out catastrophic outcomes where human life as we know it is threatened . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; So was Greta right ? Do Canadians want to contribute to these catastrophic outcomes ? Do we want to keep those well-paying jobs at Teck Resources ? Or do we want our children and grandchildren to grow up in a world where human life is NOT threatened ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Here in Ottawa , we are experiencing one of the warmest winters ever . " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;70059;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1564124 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Protest shows we need parliamentary reform&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;Re : &lt;a href= " https : **33;70089;TOOLONG ... Blockades -- It 's a no-win situation , police say , Feb. 18. &lt;/a&gt; &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The fact that so many citizens are turning to extra-parliamentary measures to secure political objectives is surely a sign that our legislative institutions are not functioning as well as they should . How far we have moved from 2003 , when then-prime minister Paul Martin was talking about the &amp;ldquo ; democratic deficit , &amp;rdquo ; to the situation today where there is not even a designated federal minister for Democratic Institutions . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Clearly , action is needed on parliamentary reform , including a reformed Senate , to provide for better representation of Indigenous Peoples . It has been almost 30 years since the Consensus Report on the Constitution ( the Charlottetown Accord ) recommended that Aboriginal representation be guaranteed in the Senate by adding Aboriginal Senate seats over and above provincial and territorial seats . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; As former senator Dan Hays has said , it may not be too late to fix the roof before it really starts raining . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Gary W. O&amp;rsquo ; Brien , Nepean , &amp;nbsp ; former Clerk of the Senate &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Indigenous protests capture larger problems too&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Indigenous complaints have been simmering for many years and now seem to have reached a boiling point . These concerns are nationwide , and common complaints unite the different groups . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There is , however , another factor at work . At the Wet&amp;rsquo ; suwet&amp;rsquo ; en protest that took place in Ottawa , one recurring theme was concern for the water that would be harmed by the Coastal GasLink pipeline . There were also concerns about the damage to the land . It is this fear of environmental damage and a coming climate crisis that especially unites the young with Indigenous People . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There is a growing realization that the present system of governance is not working in Canada . We seem to need a new story . We need to think outside of the box . The swirling union of Indigenous people and youth may be creating a new outlook for us. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Eric Schiller , Ottawa &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Lock them up ; build the pipelines&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The problem should be solved by declaring the War Measures Act : Put all those protesters in jail , with no recourse to the courts . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Build the pipelines and let them out later . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Roly Armitage , Dunrobin &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;It 's an insurrection , not a protest&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Given the total absence of leadership shown by the prime minister over the last few weeks , it would be appropriate to take up a collection to buy him a fiddle and toga . Edgecombe , Orl&amp;eacute ; ans &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Protesters are taking sides in an internal Wet'suet'en struggle&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The people who are blocking our trains , bridges and highways believe that they are supporting the Wet&amp;rsquo ; suwet&amp;rsquo ; en people . In fact , they have ( perhaps unwittingly ) taken sides in an internal Wet&amp;rsquo ; suwet&amp;rsquo ; en dispute . &amp;nbsp ; While one group of Wet&amp;rsquo ; suwet&amp;rsquo ; en leaders is trying to block the construction of a pipeline , other elected leaders support the construction and have signed agreements to advance the work . When demonstrators support one group of Wet&amp;rsquo ; suwet&amp;rsquo ; en people , they are undermining the other group . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The rights to the traditional territory of the Wet&amp;rsquo ; suwet&amp;rsquo ; en people do not belong to either group of leaders ; they belong to the Wet&amp;rsquo ; suwet&amp;rsquo ; en now living in that territory . One group was elected by some of those people but they were elected to deal with local issues and their views on broader policies may not be those of a majority of today&amp;rsquo ; s Wet&amp;rsquo ; suwet&amp;rsquo ; en . The other group claims territorial responsibility but its membership was determined by a system put in place thousands of years ago . Most nations have changed their system of government many times in that period and have moved away from hereditary power transfers . The Wet&amp;rsquo ; suwet&amp;rsquo ; en people have not had that opportunity . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The government and the construction company are now caught trying to satisfy two disagreeing groups of leaders ; neither group truly represents today&amp;rsquo ; s&amp;nbsp ; Wet&amp;rsquo ; suwe ... on this issue . The&amp;nbsp ; Wet&amp;rsquo ; suwet&amp;rsquo ; en people should be given a chance to choose leaders to represent them on this issue and the rest of us should support their right to do that . If we are truly supportive of the rights of the&amp;nbsp ; Wet&amp;rsquo ; suwet&amp;rsquo ; en ... we will not take sides in their internal dispute . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; David Lorge Parnas , Ottawa &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;70124;TOOLONG ... title= " Conservative Party leader Andrew Scheer : The prime minister should n't brush him aside , a reader says . I personally experienced the acute care hospital situation : My husband , with a fractured hip , waited on an ambulance gurney with two paramedics , along with 12 other teams , on a weekday before even seeing the triage nurse . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The answer would appear to be given in a recent &lt;a href= " https : **33;70495;TOOLONG ... CBC report on how &lt;/a&gt; Sunnybrook Hospital tackles hallway medicine by strengthening home supports . Another earlier Citizen article reported on a study being done in Renfrew , using paramedics to triage patients in the home and offer first aid for those deemed not to need hospital care . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Clearly we need to be building community care ( assessment , and supports ) rather than boosting adult care home and long-term care staffing and beds. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Jill Courtemanche , Ottawa &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;An act of kindness , remembered forever&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A profound thank you to a complete stranger for a random act of kindness one year ago today , Feb. 23 , on the Rideau Canal . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A gentleman sitting on a bench across from us noticed my husband , Randy , struggling to take a selfie of us , and offered his help . Randy had surprised me with a weekend away in Ottawa to celebrate my birthday and check off the first thing on my bucket list , to skate on the canal . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; On our Sunday evening drive home , we talked and laughed about the wonderful time we&amp;rsquo ; d had . Randy said he&amp;rsquo ; d remember this weekend for as long as he lived . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Tragically , 36 hours later , my husband was killed instantly in a car accident . The last photo I have of us together was &amp;ldquo ; gifted&amp;rdquo ; me by this stranger through a beautiful random act of kindness . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; From the bottom of my heart , I thank you . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Sally Vivian , Lindsay , Ont. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;70530;TOOLONG ... title= " Sally Vivian and her husband , Randy , on the canal in 2019 . " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;70566;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1564122 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Words matter when it comes to suicide&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;Re : &lt;a href= " https : **33;70596;TOOLONG ... Carleton student leaders call for mental health investments following another death by suicide , &lt;/a&gt; Feb. 14. &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Words matter . To those struggling to process the news of recent suicide deaths on university campuses and to those thinking about suicide , we want to say : I am here to listen . There is hope . You matter and you can get through this . For those who don&amp;rsquo ; t know what to say , say these words . If you think someone is struggling , trust your instinct . Ask in a caring and compassionate way if they are having thoughts of suicide . It won&amp;rsquo ; t put the idea in their head . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Evidence shows that high-profile responses to suicide deaths in the media , on social media , and in our personal networks can be triggering . It can remind people of their own scary feelings and thoughts . In this context , as a community we have a collective responsibility to provide and share information in a way that motivates people to seek help , and that respects families living through a terrible tragedy . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; If you are struggling , know that there are mental health walk-ins throughout Ottawa for both youth and adults . People who are over 18 can go to the &lt;a href= " https : **33;70631;TOOLONG ... Counselling Clinic Ottawa . &lt;/a&gt; Youth under 18 and their caregivers can access the &lt;a href= " https : **33;70666;TOOLONG ... Services Bureau 's walk-in clinic&lt;/a&gt; , and children under 12 and their families can access &lt;a href= " https : //crossroadschildren.ca/ " &gt;Cros ... Children 's Mental Health Centre&lt;/a&gt; , and all without a referral . In fact , for young people under 18 there are walk-in clinics across Ontario that don&amp;rsquo ; t require a physician&amp;rsquo ; s referral . If needed , these services can help people navigate to other services in the community . Knowing this and sharing that young people can get help , and that there is help , is vital . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; If you are struggling and you need help now , contact the Distress Centre 613-238-3311 or go to your nearest emergency room . " data-portal-copyright= " Luke Hendry " **28;71203;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1559268 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Do n't call my medals ' bling ' &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;Re : &lt;a href= " https : **33;71233;TOOLONG ... -- Shorter skirts , disaster relief and highlighting medals as ' bling ' might bring more women in&lt;/a&gt; , Feb. 10. &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; How disheartening it was to read David Pugliese&amp;rsquo ; s article on how to attract more women into the Canadian military . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Some in the military had initially suggested promoting medals as &amp;ldquo ; bling&amp;rdquo ; &amp;ndash ; which would certainly have lessened the pride felt from any medal earned . I served for 20 years and earned my medal when deployed to Rwanda . I would have been extremely offended if at any time it were referred to as &amp;ldquo ; bling. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Wearing a shorter , more fitted skirt will not have women flocking to recruiting centres ; most of the time , combats are the dress of the day . These ideas couldn&amp;rsquo ; t be any more sexist . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; If you want to entice women into the military , start by asking the ones already serving what changes they would like to see . Promote the technical trades ; opportunities for career progression ( it still seems male-dominated ) ; affordable base housing , especially for the junior ranks ; free parking at their place of employment when posted to places such as Ottawa ; longer postings in one place for those who may want more stability once they start families ; financial child care assistance for single parents with preschool children . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; These are a few suggestions . " data-portal-copyright= " EDWARD WANG " **28;72136;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1558727 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Let 's think more widely about infectious diseases&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The continuous media reports about the coronavirus are both a stark reminder of the danger posed by communicable diseases and a reminder of how human resolve can curb their transmission . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It is important , however , to remind ourselves that many more pathogens threaten our lives : &amp;nbsp ; pneumococcal , rotavirus , meningococcal and so on . &amp;nbsp ; The good news is that , unlike what is happening with the coronavirus , there are cheap , proven vaccines available . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; First , we need to eradicate the one pathogen that is near elimination : polio . Canada has been asked to continue its historic contribution , to the tune of $215 million over the four years required for eradication ( barely more than $1 per Canadian per year ) . It must answer in the affirmative as soon as possible . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In addition , Gavi , the global alliance that deals with all other vaccines , is currently replenishing its fund this year . &amp;nbsp ; It believes it can save an additional seven million lives if the alliance is properly funded . &amp;nbsp ; Let&amp;rsquo ; s hope that , in the current context of pandemics , Canada will contribute even more generously than in previous years . &amp;nbsp ; Not only is it the right thing to do , but , who knows , it might even help our prime minister is his bid for a seat on the UN Security Council . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Jean-Fran&amp;ccedil ; ois Tardif , Gatineau &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Open our church doors to the homeless&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;Re : &lt;a href= " https : **33;72166;TOOLONG ... Psalm 12:5 says , &amp;ldquo ; Because the poor are plundered , because the needy groan , I will now arise , &amp;rdquo ; say the Lord ; &amp;ldquo ; I will place him in the safety for which he longs . &amp;rdquo ; John 3:17 says , &amp;ldquo ; If anyone has material possessions and sees a brother or sister in need but has no pity on them , how can the love of God be in that person ? &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Churches are plentiful , warm and have a great deal of space ; they would be ideal to shelter those in need . They preach goodness and generosity , and have many parishioners who presumably share those values . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Food for thought methinks &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Nancy Thayer , Nepean &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;How to clean up the ByWash&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;Re : &lt;a href= " https : **33;72201;TOOLONG ... blame stagnant water for foul smell at Rideau Station , says hydrology expert&lt;/a&gt; , Feb. 8. &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Prominent hydrology expert Slobodan Simonovic , from Western University , commenting on the foul odour of &amp;ldquo ; rotten eggs&amp;rdquo ; assailing LRT riders at Rideau Station , has correctly rebutted the hypothesis that it arises from stagnant water because of the depth of water mobility through soil at 26.5 metres , the deepest site on the Confederation line . In response , local historian Andrew King made reference to remnants of the ByWash , winding generally north through the ByWard Market area that was covered over to form a sewer in 1872 , a likely source of the contamination . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In recent years , members of at least two local historical societies undertook extensive investigation of the route of the ByWash . One source was a March 21 , 1876 Citizen news story published in the NCC publication Lower Town Ottawa , Vol. 2 , 1854-1900 , authored by the late historian Michael Newton . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Col. John By constructed the ByWash circa 1828 beginning as an overflow sluice gate on the northeast side of the former Rideau Canal Turning Basin , thence in a northeast direction across Rideau Street to George Street , through the ByWard Market along York Street and down King Street ( King Edward ) to the Rideau River following the course of an existing creek bed draining the cedar swamp of Lowertown. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Related details and stories have been published in the community newspaper , The Lowertown Echo ( Nov. -Dec , 2018 , p. 6 ) as The By Wash : Lowertown&amp;rsquo ; s Venetian Canal and Under the City : A Trip Through the Main Sewer of ( 1876 ) , an abridged version of the hilarious 1876 Citizen saga of a sewer excursion by city officials of that time when the sewer had just opened . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Presumably the site of the 1875 Rideau St. manhole sewer entrance ( roughly in the area northeast of the Rideau Centre and LRT Station ) could be located today and injected with oxidizing agents to treat the foul festering mess of the past 150 years . The perfect agent for treatment would be industrial grade 30 per cent hydrogen peroxide in water that reduces to harmless water on decomposition , preferable to Javelle water ( **25;72236;TOOLONG acid stabilized with sodium hydroxide ) whose chloride ion content would be corrosive to any iron-based infrastructure within the catchment area . One must take care now not to contribute to any further deterioration of the already embattled LRT infrastructure . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; George Neville , Ottawa , retired chemist and past president , The Historical Society of Ottawa &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;LRT 's foul odour often found in wells&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The source of the foul odour associated with the LRT Station is highly likely sulphur from ground water , which is very common in this area . &amp;nbsp ; I am a well contractor and see this condition in most wells in this region . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Barry Wedd , Carp &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;nbsp ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN THE NEWS&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;72263;TOOLONG ...
