The fact is that private health coverage for Canadians and their families has increased &amp;ndash ; to 80 per cent of working Canadians from 73 per cent a decade ago . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; These private plans offer better and more comprehensive coverage than public plans . For example , a Conference Board study just last week found that nearly 10 per cent of arthritis drugs prescribed are not accessible through public plans , yet are covered through private insurance . If Canadians were required to give up their private plans and move to a public plan , more than seven million would lose access to medications they are currently taking . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Canadians understand this , which is why the latest survey data show that 80 per cent of those with group benefits want the federal government to focus on those who aren&amp;rsquo ; t covered through a workplace or public plan ; even 60 per cent of those without coverage agree . Cardinal &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Fix for long-term care crisis wo n't be easy&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;Re : &lt;a href= " https : **33;73359;TOOLONG ... can help solve the crisis in long-term care&lt;/a&gt; , Feb. 3. &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; While it makes sense to involve family doctors more in the planning of their patients&amp;rsquo ; home care and possible placement in long-term care , it isn&amp;rsquo ; t going to resolve the crisis in long-term care in Ontario . &amp;nbsp ; &amp;nbsp ; Long-term care homes are unable to retain and recruit personal support workers ( PSW ) ; the high resident-to-staff ratio means that the workload is too heavy , the compensation is too low and there is too much part-time work without benefits . Colleges can&amp;rsquo ; t fill spaces in their PSW programs even when offering free tuition . &amp;nbsp ; &amp;nbsp ; Most long-term care homes regularly work short-staffed , putting their residents and staff at risk of serious injury . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The wait lists for long-term care beds across Ontario are growing at alarming rates , up 78 per cent between 2011-2012 and 2018-2019 . There are now more than 36,000 frail , sick seniors waiting for a bed while their families and friends struggle to supplement the limited home care available to their loved ones . Many of those waiting for care are occupying much-needed hospital beds . Since 2013 , only 84 new long-term care beds have been built in Ottawa despite the city&amp;rsquo ; s aging population . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Addressing the long-term crisis in Ontario is going to take government investment in human and physical resources , combined with innovative ideas . It is not going to be easy to fix long-term care but if they can build a hospital in 10 days in China , can&amp;rsquo ; t we find solutions ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Grace Welch , Chair , Advocacy Committee , Champlain Region Family Council Network &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;If we want better schools and hospitals , we have to pay up&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;Re : &lt;a href= " https : **33;73394;TOOLONG ... much to meet teachers ' demands&lt;/a&gt; ? Liberal governments are past masters at borrowing and practise it until even they recognize its limits . Perhaps something to think about ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Nona Nalley , Orl&amp;eacute ; ans &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Here 's what the Greenbelt is for&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;Re : &lt;a href= " https : **33;73464;TOOLONG ... is our failed attempt to contain growth , &lt;/a&gt; Feb. 5. &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Greenbelt was never intended to contain growth . Instead , Jacques Gr&amp;eacute ; ber&amp;rsquo ; s vision was that satellite cities would develop outside the Greenbelt . But successive city planners allowed development outside the Greenbelt to function largely as car-dependent bedroom communities . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Only now ( five decades later ) is the current crop of planners coming around to the insight that the suburbs east , south and west of the centre should become &amp;ldquo ; complete communities . &amp;rdquo ; Indeed , in a desperate attempt to wean Ottawa off car dependency , they are advocating so-called &amp;ldquo ; 15-minute neighbourhoods&amp;rdquo ; everywhere , in the suburbs as well as in the core . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Better late than never . Meanwhile , the Greenbelt &amp;ndash ; the lungs of our urban area &amp;ndash ; should be left alone . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Erwin Dreessen , Ottawa &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Do n't sacrifice the Greenbelt&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I read with great interest Randall Denley&amp;rsquo ; s piece about developing certain parts of the Greenbelt to address our climate emergency . While I agree that urban sprawl is an important environmental issue , developing natural areas , farmland and other green space is not the solution . The city needs to look at densification in existing neighbourhoods and stand up to developers that keep pushing for urban boundary expansions to build single family homes . There is plenty of land for infill development &amp;ndash ; within the current urban boundary and without sacrificing green space &amp;ndash ; to meet our housing needs for years , if not decades . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Greenbelt provides countless ecological goods and services , including various natural solutions to climate change ; the capture and storage of carbon ; temperature regulation ; flood and drought mitigation and local , sustainably produced food . If anything , the Greenbelt should be preserved , protected and showcased as a model for other urban areas . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Greenbelt also contains the most biodiverse place in eastern Ontario : Mer Bleue ! There are some 75 schools within a one-kilometre radius of the Greenbelt &amp;ndash ; talk about potential for connecting children and their families to nature , sustainable agriculture and recreation opportunities . The areas between the &amp;ldquo ; gems&amp;rdquo ; of the Greenbelt , Mer Bleue , Stony Swamp , Greens Creek and Shirley&amp;rsquo ; s Bay are just as important . What people may see as scrubland or farmers&amp;rsquo ; fields are , in fact , important habitats in and of themselves and provide connectivity between core natural areas , thereby allowing wildlife to move around the core . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; We can reconcile the housing needs of a growing city with conservation of nature and build a capital that is green and dynamic . Let&amp;rsquo ; s protect the Greenbelt . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; John McDonnell , Executive Director , Canadian Parks and Wilderness Society &amp;ndash ; Ottawa Valley Chapter &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Homeless matter more than libraries&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;Re : &lt;a href= " https : **33;73499;TOOLONG ... left in the cold after library locks foyer , says councillor&lt;/a&gt; , Feb. 5. &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I read with interest the story that the library is not available 24/7 to the homeless in Ottawa . Or , as a modern-day philosopher said , &amp;ldquo ; If the brain were so simple we could understand it , we&amp;rsquo ; d be so simple that we couldn&amp;rsquo ; t. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Peter Johnson , Carp &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Nuclear waste : all talk and studies&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Re : &lt;a href= " https : **33;73534;TOOLONG ... over Ontario nuclear waste disposal reveals hurdle in Liberal 's climate plan&lt;/a&gt; , Feb. 3. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ontario Power Generation&amp;rsquo ; s plans for storing low-level radioactive wastes at Bruce have hit a roadblock . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Here we go again &amp;hellip ; or don&amp;rsquo ; t . In 1986 , the nuclear regulator , then called the Atomic Energy Control Board , required Eldorado Nuclear to find a safer storage for low-level but long-lived radioactive wastes from refinery operations near Port Hope , Ontario . Eldorado came up with a proposal to put the wastes in geological formations roughly similar to what Ontario Power is proposing . This was technically a very good proposal , as is Ontario Power&amp;rsquo ; s , but public reaction and the NIMBY syndrome caused the government to intervene , take control of the wastes and commit to finding a &amp;ldquo ; willing&amp;rdquo ; site . The government even offered unspecified benefits to the willing site . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ten years later , no willing site could be found except Deep River at the Chalk River site , but the government would not pay the asked-for benefits . Now those wastes are still in the Port Hope area but in a better on-surface facility than the original , but which is less safe and secure than what Eldorado Nuclear proposed in the first place . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It looks like the same sort of thing might happen at Bruce . The wastes will remain in the &amp;ldquo ; back yard&amp;rdquo ; of the Saugeen Ojibway Nation but in a less safe and secure facility , requiring perpetual human monitoring . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Tom Spears is right on the mark when he writes that no big project can be built &amp;ldquo ; &amp;hellip ; without making some people very upset . &amp;rdquo ; Judging from the remarks of Chief Lester Anoquot , it appears that the Saugeen Ojibway Nation will want some sort of benefit in return for their consent . Very wily of him . If I were Ontario Power , I would leave the wastes where they are ( they are safe enough for the present ) and let the government take care of it along with nuclear fuel wastes . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; So far the only action by successive governments on nuclear fuel wastes is a lot of talk and &lt;br&gt; studies , even though the technology has been available for decades . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; John Beare , Kanata &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;There 's light at the end of the infill tunnel&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;Re : Temper flares after two years of upheaval on Hintonburg street , Jan. 31. &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ah yes , infill construction issues . Now STO ( Gatineau&amp;rsquo ; s equivalent to OC Transpo ) is considering LRT options for future rapid transit &amp;ndash ; an evolution from its current Rapi-Bus system . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; However , it is the proposed connection to Ottawa&amp;rsquo ; s LRT line that is raising eyebrows . The STO is proposing to run its LRT across the Portage bridge to connect to Ottawa&amp;rsquo ; s Confederation Line , instead of the Prince of Wales rail bridge , which connects to both the Confederation Line and the Trillium Line at Bayview LRT Station . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; These are proposals which have yet to go through an environmental assessment process on the various options but it will certainly have an impact on the NCC&amp;rsquo ; s plans for LeBreton Flats . As well , there is an underlying assumption that Ottawa would be a contributor to the costs of this connection . Which Ottawa &amp;ndash ; the city or the federal government &amp;ndash ; hasn&amp;rsquo ; t been determined . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The timing is auspicious , however , as the City of Ottawa is currently reviewing its Transportation Master Plan , of which the Prince of Wales bridge is a significant factor for future cross-river transportation . As well , the NCC is &amp;ldquo ; refreshing&amp;rdquo ; its inter-provincial bridge study at the same time . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The stars have yet to align , but at least there is movement . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Alex Cullen , Ottawa &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;A wheelchair user ( happily ) rides the train&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I understand the frustrations many riders have with LRT , but I&amp;rsquo ; d like to share my own positive thoughts . As a user of a power wheelchair , I find the LRT experience much easier and less stressful than taking the bus . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; No more having to wait for the next bus because the priority seats were full &amp;ndash ; plenty of those on the LRT . No more waiting for the ramp to come down , watching the driver having to get up and pull it down when it doesn&amp;rsquo ; t work , or holding my breath as someone , oblivious to the beeping sound and descending ramp , walks in front of it . Now just drive onto the LRT , level with the platform . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; No more navigating along the narrow aisle and manoeuvring around the passengers , strollers and walkers in the front seats &amp;ndash ; lots of space on the LRT . No more turning my chair to get out &amp;ndash ; on the LRT , drive in one door and out the other . An added bonus on the LRT : I can face the direction it&amp;rsquo ; s going . There are two large elevators at every station , instead of only one cramped one , which means I no longer have to check beforehand to see if it&amp;rsquo ; s working . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Admittedly , if I had to ride the train during busy times , my overall experience might be different , but for all the reasons above , I&amp;rsquo ; m a very happy LRT rider . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Jean Grant , Ottawa &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Commute to Algonquin is **27;73829;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; My concern is the commute my adult children face in going to Algonquin . A 10-cent surcharge on bottled water and pop will curb consumption and force manufacturers to find alternatives . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Shame on anyone not using cloth bags . Let&amp;rsquo ; s get back to drinking tap water and making our own coffee at home or the office . Time spent washing a cup is time spent saving the planet . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Mildred Beechey , Ottawa &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;How ordinary people can make a difference on climate change&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Although the majority of Canadians understand the science of climate change , we are unwilling to make the changes required to drastically reduce CO2 emissions . Canadians have a fossil fuel addiction and like giving up tobacco , it&amp;rsquo ; s very difficult to break our habit . Canada can&amp;rsquo ; t continue with the status quo as this will result in more floods , tornadoes , and a longer forest fire season like what we are watching with horror in Australia . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; How can ordinary citizens make a difference ? Make our homes more energy-efficient ; eat less meat ; take public transit ; walk and cycle more ; own a fuel-efficient car , or better yet an electric vehicle ; install solar panels on our roofs ; use less energy ( turn off the lights and turn down the thermostat ) ; divest from fossil fuels in our investment portfolios ; talk to friends and family about climate change and how it affects our lives ; fly less ; and vote for leaders who actually have a serious , credible policy to reduce emissions . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Canadians must pull our weight when it comes to climate action as we did in the two world wars . Indigenous people , who represent about five per cent of Canada&amp;rsquo ; s population , now make up 30 per cent of the population in federal prisons ; four years ago the number was 25 per cent . The ombudsman , speaking only for federal prisons , said the pace is now set for Indigenous people to make up 33 per cent of the federal prison population in the next three years . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Supreme Court of Canada established more than 10 years ago what has become known as the Gladue Court . That ruling called upon judges to exercise discretion and to be sensitive to the historical plight of aboriginal people and to consider aboriginal heritage &amp;ndash ; through Gladue reports &amp;ndash ; when sentencing native offenders . The overall response by the judiciary to that landmark ruling , as evidenced by the growing incarceration rate of aboriginals , has been apathetic and sporadic . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Despite the Gladue decision in 1999 , some jurisdictions across the country have considered/interpreted it only to be a suggestion . But in April , 2012 in the Ipeela case , the Supreme Court reiterated it was a firm requirement . &amp;ldquo ; Application of the Gladue principles is required in every case involving an aboriginal offender , &amp;rdquo ; Supreme Court Justice Louis LeBel wrote . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; One expert , Jonathan Rudin , program director of Aboriginal Legal Services of Toronto , has argued this situation could &amp;nbsp ; lead to a standoff between the courts and the government . Is the Gladue court just another failed initiative ? Time for the federal government &amp;nbsp ; to step up to the plate and make the Gladue court a reality right across the country . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Emile Therien , Ottawa , public health and safety advocate &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Federal Conservatives are linguistically **25;74760;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;Re : &lt;a href= " https : **33;74787;TOOLONG ... bilingualism myths die hard -- just ask Peter MacKay , &lt;/a&gt; Jan. 28&lt;/em&gt; . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; From Andrew Scheer to Peter MacKay and the other announced candidates for the Conservative Party leadership , despite ample available time and resources , the comically declining ability to learn French does not give me confidence in that party . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; On the other hand , the laughable but tragic mangling of the French language in MacKay&amp;rsquo ; s announcement has restored my own confidence in navigating a bilingual world with my level of French . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Merci et bonne chance , Monsieur MacKay ! &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Thomas Brawn , Orl&amp;eacute ; ans &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Not all anti-Soviet fighters were war criminals&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;Re : &lt;a href= " https : **33;74822;TOOLONG ... groups warn about Nazi glorification in Eastern European nations&lt;/a&gt; , Jan. 26. &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The celebration of men and units involved in the Holocaust is clearly of serious concern . The history of the Second World War in the East is generally misunderstood in the West . By way of context , as an ally of Nazi Germany , the Soviet Union invaded Finland and eastern Poland in 1939 and Lithuania , Estonia and Latvia in 1940 . The U.S.S.R. brutally repressed the local populations , as the Soviets had previously terrorized , murdered and starved ethnic Ukrainians in the Holomodor . As a result , and as shocking as it may seem to us now , when the Germans attacked the U.S.S.R. in 1941 many Latvians , Lithuanians , Estonians and Ukrainians saw the invaders as liberators. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Pro-German local units engaged in pogroms and were heavily complicit in the Holocaust . The local population was also organized into formal military units that were sent to the front &amp;ndash ; often in SS uniform . While apologists for these military groups allege that all the members of these units were simple soldiers , men often moved between front-line units and behind-the-lines &amp;ldquo ; security&amp;rdquo ; forces ( who murdered Jews and suspected partisans ) . To add to the confusion , the war in the East was blurred by conflicting loyalties and goals : for example , Bandera&amp;rsquo ; s Ukrainian Insurgent Army ( mentioned in David Pugliese&amp;rsquo ; s article ) variously fought the Soviets , the Nazis and the Polish Home Army , as local alliances and power dynamics shifted . Mass mobilization for the Estonian SS did not even occur until 1944 ; by this time it was clear that Germany had lost the war but many Estonians were terrified of renewed Soviet occupation . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; After the war , the Allies took the position that Nazi collaborators in the East were driven by various motives , including anti-communism and a desire for national independence . For this reason , members of the Baltic SS units were not considered by the Allies to be as problematic , or as Nazified , as other members of the SS . Former Estonian SS men , this time in U.S. uniform , even guarded the Nazi defendants during the post-war Nuremberg trials . Of course , the Allied official approach to the Baltic SS units whitewashed the murderous activities of certain of its members , including Viktors Arajs and his infamous kommando. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The horror of the Holocaust is clear , stark and undeniable and those responsible should be identified and pilloried , not glorified . But the complexity of the Second World War in the East ( which could be seen as half a dozen simultaneous wars with overlapping participants ) militates against adopting the Kremlin&amp;rsquo ; s highly political argument that all anti-Soviet fighters were necessarily war criminals . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Andrew Mason , Ottawa &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN OPINION&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;74857;TOOLONG ... " data-portal-copyright= " NORA SAVOSNICK " **28;75074;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1548430 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Canada has n't fully acknowledged its anti-semitic past&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;Re : Jewish Groups Worry About Nazi Glorification in Eastern Europe , Jan. 25. &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Canada has its own skeletons in the closet as regards anti-semitism . Just over 20 years ago , an attempt by the Canadian War Museum in Ottawa to tell the Holocaust story fully , including Canada&amp;rsquo ; s own record of prejudice , was defeated by various opponents including historians reluctant to see the anti-semitism of the wartime Mackenzie King government highlighted . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In a fitting irony , when a monument to the Holocaust was finally built in Ottawa , it rose next door to the new war museum , a permanently visible reminder of shameful moral failure and a story still waiting to be told . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Victor Suthren , former director general , Canadian War Museum ( 1986-1997 ) &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;nbsp ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Speak out against honouring Nazis&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Bravo to the Ottawa Citizen and David Pugliese for highlighting that some eastern European countries are honouring Nazi collaborators as heroes . It is frightening that Latvia , Ukraine and Lithuania , in an attempt to rebuild their national identities , are &amp;ldquo ; rediscovering&amp;rdquo ; certain individuals from the 1940s to elevate to national hero status . These individuals fought against the Russians for independence , but also joined forces with the Nazis , and murdered Jews . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There have been parades to honour Ukrainian Stepan Bandera , a Nazi collaborator . A Lithuanian school has been named for Jonas Noreika , a nationalist leader whose family acknowledged he was involved in killing Jews . Last year , Global Affairs Canada condemned the parades in Latvia to honour the Latvian SS . Some parade participants wore swastikas and Nazi insignias . Sadly Canadian officials did not join Jewish groups in condemning Latvian Defence Minister Artis Pabrik&amp;rsquo ; s praise of the Latvian SS. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Our federal government needs to stand up and push back with greater resolve against the growing glorification of Nazi hatred . It seems that the LRT lemon is likely to get larger . A sobering consideration indeed . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; After a morning of solemn rumination , I thought : &amp;rdquo ; There must be a way for us to make some lemonade &amp;ndash ; but how ? &amp;rdquo ; Well , how about an LRT Lottery ? The Trillium Line extension , TransitNEXT , offers many ways to play . Given the poor technical and shockingly &amp;ldquo ; good&amp;rdquo ; financial scores achieved by SNC-Lavalin , a number of possibilities come to mind . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Cost overrun : Will the Trillium Line construction cost $663 million ? Perhaps city Auditor General Ken Hughes could keep track and proclaim the new , improved amount frequently . LRT Lotto players could try to estimate the revised total . A cash prize would go to the person whose guess is closest to the actual &amp;ldquo ; cost upgrade . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Completion date : Players could prognosticate the date the Trillium Extension will actually be up and running . Chances are this will still be a part of the lottery action well past the proposed 2022 completion . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Technical glitches : Once the line is fully operational , players could try their hand at predicting what will go wrong next &amp;ndash ; and when . We&amp;rsquo ; ve all had ample opportunity to develop expertise in this area since the fall of 2019 . This part of the lottery has the potential to last forever . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The greater good : As well as providing the opportunity for at least some of us to become richer , monies raised could be used to cover , at least in part , the above mentioned cost overrun . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; One daily benefit : The latest news concerning winners in the various divisions ( overruns , delays and what&amp;rsquo ; s wrong now ? ) could be broadcast on the screens at the front of all buses . " data-portal-copyright= " Jean Levac " **28;75784;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1547193 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;What sort of steel did they use on LRT wheels ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Re : &lt;a href= " https : **33;75814;TOOLONG ... details LRT 's winter woes&lt;/a&gt; , Jan. 24. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Michael Morgan , the city&amp;rsquo ; s rail construction director , has said there needs to be a root-cause investigation to see why so many wheel flats have been happening lately to LRT train units . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Could it be that Alstom used a softer steel for its LRT wheels than that for the SNC rails , thereby inducing flattening on braking ? The flattening effect would be much greater under the fully loaded conditions of this winter&amp;rsquo ; s operation compared to the unloaded testing throughout last winter . What are the hardness specifications of the present wheels and rails respectively , and what original steel hardness specifications were cited for each in the contract requirements ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The time-consuming operation of removing flat sectors on steel wheels by shaving ( reducing ) a portion of wheel diameters using a double-wheel lathe for the purpose ( not initially operational ) results in progressively smaller diameter wheels with shorter lifetimes due to them having to rotate faster and more times to match the traction of the original diameter wheels . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; George Neville , Ottawa &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;The wheel problem seems unique to Ottawa&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The problem with LRT wheels should be an embarrassment to all LRT &lt;br&gt; **28;75849;TOOLONG people . Train wheels&amp;rsquo ; flat-spotting implies very poor build and/or absent , inadequate or ignored specs. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; This LRT-wheels chronic problem is new . it was not a part of the learning-curve evolution of rail technology . There have been hundreds of thousands ( maybe millions ) of rail rolling-stock units in this country , for more than 100 years . &lt;br&gt; With no chronic wheel problems . So why are these new LRT units&amp;rsquo ; wheels &lt;br&gt; deficient ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Wheel flat-spots are from brakes locking up the wheels , or from poor steel in the wheels , or from under-design . Older engineers typically included fudge-factors in their designs to compensate for what they did not know . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Brian Boyd , P. Eng . ( retired ) , Orl&amp;eacute ; ans &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Hire Byford to fix LRT mess&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; If the City of Ottawa is serious about fixing the problems with the Confederation Line LRT , it should hire Andy Byford to get the job done . Mr. Byford , who &lt;a href= " https : **33;75879;TOOLONG ... just resigned&lt;/a&gt; as the head of the New York City Transit Authority , is one of the world&amp;rsquo ; s leading public transit turnaround specialists . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He raised the on-time performance of New York&amp;rsquo ; s massive subway system from 58 to 80 per cent in just two years , and under his leadership the Toronto Transportation Commission was named the best public transit agency in North America . Now that Byford is looking for his next opportunity , our city leaders would be smart to snap him up . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Vivek Krishnamurthy , Ottawa &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Police services board faces real **25;75914;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The next meeting of the Board of the Ottawa Police Services ( OPS ) is today , Jan. 27. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; As a former member of this board for six years ( three as vice-chair ) , I can attest to the heavy workload that membership of the OPS board entails , even during unexceptional times . Currently the service is undergoing a significant transformation in its senior leadership . The chief and the CAO are new . One of the two deputy chiefs is under investigation . &amp;nbsp ; The service is searching for a new CFO. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The board itself has also recently undergone a major transformation , leaving only two &amp;ldquo ; legacy&amp;rdquo ; members from the previous membership going forward . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The issues that arise will be challenging for a board composed of mostly new members learning their roles , a mayor who has already more than enough on his plate and a member on ongoing medical leave of absence . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Provincial law requires the board to elect a chair and a vice-chair from within its membership at its first meeting each year . I hope that the board will take these matters into serious consideration when choosing its own leadership for the next 12 months . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Jacques Legendre , Ottawa , former OPS Board member ( 1997-2003 , former vice-chair 2000 to 2003 ) &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;nbsp ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN THE NEWS&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;75941;TOOLONG ... Cross , Ottawa &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Bring back the express buses&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;Re : &lt;a href= " https : **33;76228;TOOLONG ... the car ! When LRT pushes us back behind the wheel&lt;/a&gt; , Jan. 23. &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Thank you , Kelly Egan , for putting the whole LRT situation into one comprehensive article . As always , I rely on your telling of our city&amp;rsquo ; s tales to get the accurate story and put matters into perspective . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I am a public transit user and have been in Ottawa since we moved here from Montreal in 1998 . As a child in 1967 , I watched my home city implement a Metro system in time for Expo 67 that , to this day , is running efficiently and effectively . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It is a major stressor to wake up in the morning and worry about whether I am going to get to work on time . In the &amp;ldquo ; old days&amp;rdquo ; ( pre-LRT ) , I used to get on the express bus on the corner of my residential street in Orl&amp;eacute ; ans and get off at Metcalfe Street , walking two blocks to my office , all in under 45 minutes . Now I take that same bus to Blair station , where I have to disembark , get on a train , get off at Parliament Station , go up two very long escalators and a flight of stairs , then walk the equivalent of five blocks to get to my office , in 60 to 90 minutes . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; This is not progress . My suggestion is that the city bring back the dual system of buses and the train , at the very least at peak periods . &amp;nbsp ; It&amp;rsquo ; s not my job that will make me retire early , but it may well be my commute . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Nancy Keirstead , Orl&amp;eacute ; ans &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Why are educational assistants facing increased problems ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;Re : &lt;a href= " https : **33;76263;TOOLONG ... educational assistants deserve proper pay , &lt;/a&gt; Jan. 22. &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The title of Smokey Thomas&amp;rsquo ; column is correct . " data-portal-copyright= " Jean Levac " **28;76334;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1546264 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Some obvious problems in Ontario 's education plan&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A couple of no-brainer observations about contract negotiations between the province and high school teachers . First , bigger class sizes inevitably lead to less effective teaching and learning . Second , the last thing teenagers need in this increasingly impersonal digital society is more screen and computer time &amp;ndash ; which is exactly what they would have with mandatory e-learning. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Too bad our provincial government has not yet managed to work its way through these no-brainers. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; David Fraser , Ottawa &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; **25;76364;TOOLONG are leading from behind&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;Re : &lt;a href= " https : **33;76391;TOOLONG ... Teachable moment , &lt;/a&gt; Jan. 18. &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The regime at Queen&amp;rsquo ; s Park mentioned in its early days that everyone must pay to reduce the principal of the provincial debt . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The article references teacher concerns about increased class sizes and secondary students taking two courses online . If the average class size moves from 22 to 25 students , that is a 12 per cent increase . Should we expect a similar percentage increase in the workload of Doug Ford&amp;rsquo ; s MPPs by having 12 per cent fewer regime MPPs ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Ford regime wants to lessen inherited deficits ( they aren&amp;rsquo ; t the fault of this government ) . Yet many of the members of the regime have been MPPs or party supporters for years . They never proposed a &amp;ldquo ; Free the Children Fund&amp;rdquo ; which would see a dedicated amount gathered annually and placed on the principal of the debt load the children will carry into the future . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The article mentions bargaining in good faith . Good faith is earned by active listening and cooperating to reach an acceptable result for all . The regime leans towards dictation or &amp;ldquo ; my way or the highway . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Teaching is a profession ; many teachers and educational assistants know that . The regime seems to see only money saved . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Yes , everyone must pay . To this point , groups such as autistic families , the poor , those wanting real climate change action are paying . If they look over their shoulders they will see their leaders not quite up to the task . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Mike FitzPatrick , Smiths Falls &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;English class is about more than reading&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;Re : &lt;a href= " https : **33;76426;TOOLONG ... course on Indigenous literature not well thought out&lt;/a&gt; , Jan. 21. &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Randall Denley believes that the goal of high school English instruction is to stimulate a love of reading . While I don&amp;rsquo ; t disagree with that point , I must argue that it is a lot more than that . Secondary English classes are about communication , which encompasses reading , speaking , writing , thinking and the sharing of ideas . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It would be wonderful if all students had a love of reading but they don&amp;rsquo ; t and it has less to do with the books that are read in class and more with the outside distractions of social media , games and TV. &amp;nbsp ; Books can&amp;rsquo ; t compete . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; If you come from a reading family , you are more likely to enjoy reading . If you were read to a lot as a child , you are more likely to be a competent reader . In fact , having been read to is the greatest indicator of overall success in elementary school . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Denley assumes that the novels read in the grade 11 Indigenous course will not be interesting . How does he know what today&amp;rsquo ; s average 16-year-old will like ? &amp;nbsp ; The most common novels in Canadian high schools are To Kill a Mockingbird and Lord of the Flies . Why is racism in 1930s&amp;rsquo ; Alabama and the brutish behaviour of British schoolboys more relevant and interesting than the experiences of Canada&amp;rsquo ; s Indigenous people ? It is time that Ontario students learned more than historical facts about native people . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I taught secondary English for more than 30 years and I still have not forgotten an off-hand remark made by a 15-year-old boy , &amp;nbsp ; who was originally from Kuwait , about &amp;ldquo ; drunken Indians . &amp;rdquo ; That pretty much says it all . To paraphrase To Kill a Mockingbird , surely one of the goals of high school English is to encourage our students to walk in another&amp;rsquo ; s shoes . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Cathy Haley , Ottawa &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; **27;76461;TOOLONG is the year to fight **29;76490;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In 2019 , the Doug Ford government made significant cuts to critical social services and programs relied upon by some of Ontario&amp;rsquo ; s most vulnerable populations . This included cuts to health care , education and a cut of $84.5 million to child welfare funding in the province . The cuts will have the effect of launching already marginalized populations into even more precarious and unsafe conditions . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; While the government has back-peddled on some its proposed cuts , there is no certainty or governmental promise that these programs will be safe from future cuts . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Consequently , given the devastating funding cuts in 2019 , and the cuts yet to come , 2020 is the year to fight back . We must push against these cuts to social programs and show the Ford government that its disregard for critical social services and the well-being of all provincial residents will not be accepted . We must come together to ensure that Ontarians continue to have access to the social services provided prior to the current government , and push for new policies and programs that improve the lives and well-being of all people in the province . We can not rest until we ensure that the basic needs of all Ontarians are met , and there are programs in place to ensure an adequate standard of living for all . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; 2019 was a year of cuts to programs and funding . 2020 can be a year for change . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Lyndsay Scovil , Ottawa &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Westboro bus crash needs proper safety **28;76521;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;Re : &lt;a href= " https : **33;76551;TOOLONG ... disaster investigations need Canada 's full attention . So far , it 's not happening&lt;/a&gt; , Jan. 20. &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; As a former aircraft accident investigator , I agree wholeheartedly with Ahmed . Shalaby&amp;rsquo ; s article . The International Civil Aviation Organization ( ICAO ) sets aircraft accident investigation standards with the goal of preventing recurrences , not assigning blame . The Canadian Transportation Safety Board ( TSB ) follows these standards . Legal proceedings may proceed concurrently , but are not supported by the TSB . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Road investigations are all about blame and are focused on &amp;ldquo ; driver error . &amp;rdquo ; In the case of the Ottawa bus tragedy , the driver has been charged but there won&amp;rsquo ; t be a trial , hence any answers , until 2021 . Even then , the answers will not likely lead to enhanced safety . A proper investigation would look at management , training , procedures for dealing with drivers who have had previous incidents and of course the design of the vehicle and the physical environment ; the findings would support safety recommendations . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Drivers and transit riders deserve to know that tragedies like this will lead to system changes that will increase the chance that they return home safely . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ross Bowie , Ottawa &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;First Nations children have immediate needs too&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;Re : &lt;a href= " https : **33;76586;TOOLONG ... to give families of crash victims $25,000 each&lt;/a&gt; , Jan. 17. &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; How generous it was of our prime minister to step up and offer more than $2 million in financial support to the 57 Canadians and 29 permanent residents of the Flight 752 tragedy . Trillium Line bid shows why Ottawa is losing trust in public **27;77087;TOOLONG &lt;/div&gt; Ottawa Citizen Editorial BoardSat , 25 Jan 2020 12:29:25 **45;77116;TOOLONG ... letters : Canada 's public service needs independent studyhttp : **35;77163;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;77200;TOOLONG ... title= " Tunney 's Pasture houses many of Canada 's public servants . But what are they really thinking ? " alt= " Tunney 's Pasture houses many of Canada 's public servants . But what are they really thinking ? " data-portal-copyright= " Jean Levac " **28;77236;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1545922 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; **25;77266;TOOLONG probe of the public service is long overdue&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Re : &lt;a href= " https : **33;77293;TOOLONG ... feeling better but there 's still work to do : survey&lt;/a&gt; , Jan. 17. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I retired from the federal public service in 2018 after 30 years . Over time , I filled out more than my share of the public service employee surveys , each as inconsequential as the next . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The problem with the survey is that its focus is far too narrow . Yes , mental health and workplace wellbeing are important . But if the public service is truly intent on attracting and retaining the best and brightest , a far more extensive and detailed review is needed . &amp;ldquo ; How do you feel ? &amp;rdquo ; questionnaires are not the answer . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I see a public service where innovative thinking has given way to institutional timidity and a culture where contrary thinking is too often deemed unhelpful and unwelcome . This is how public service goes from being an honourable calling to a debilitating grind . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Over the course of my career , I lived through several public service &amp;ldquo ; renewal&amp;rdquo ; exercises . Yet each of these suffered from the same flaw : they were nothing more than internal reviews led by senior managers themselves and as such highly unlikely to challenge the very status quo in which those same managers operated . They produced little that was new or innovative concerning key issues of recruitment , renewal , the loss of corporate memory , the public-service-political relationship and the role of the public service as a vehicle for policy innovation and advice . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The federal public service has historically been the subject of royal commissions which injected the kind of fresh thinking that only an outside perspective can bring . They provided sweeping inquiries into key public service reform issues that can not be addressed by the equivalent of a hand-holding exercise . An independent and wide-ranging examination of the federal public service is long overdue . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Such a review is essential in the face of a policy environment in which &amp;ldquo ; best practices&amp;rdquo ; have become mere code words for indecision and public service paralyzed by institutional timidity . The desire to avoid hard choices , present bad news , admit mistakes , and own to responsibility&amp;nbsp ; is what brought us the Phoenix pay system debacle . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; These are serious questions . Mr. Kopytko was a teacher in the rural one-room schoolhouses of Canal , Armstrong , Mellonville and Normand , Manitoba until 1954. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He was 16 when he finished high school and moved to the big city to attend Normal School , which was the name of what later became Winnipeg&amp;rsquo ; s Teacher&amp;rsquo ; s College . He grew up poor , a child of Ukrainian immigrants . He studied by the light of coal-oil lamps . He had 13 brothers and sisters . They ate a lot of cranberry sandwiches ( cranberry bushes grew thick around the farm ) . Once he told me that one of his favourite things about going to teacher&amp;rsquo ; s college was that on the weekends the breakfasts included bacon . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He was the youngest person ever to graduate from Normal School . He was dedicated and moved back promptly to start teaching . He did it all . I have seen his lesson plans . He taught everything : reading , writing , history , polka dance lessons , English and French . Today , travelling northwest from Winnipeg by car at highway speeds , it takes 3 &amp;frac12 ; hours to reach that district . It was hard work and he had little support . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; After four years , he quit teaching . He moved to Winnipeg and started working in a slaughterhouse . He stayed at Canada Packers folding hides and filling pails with margarine for the next 25 years . He frequently got ringworm and the loud machines caused him to lose his hearing . But he never went back to teaching . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I asked him once &amp;ldquo ; Why ? &amp;rdquo ; and all that he would say is &amp;ldquo ; I don&amp;rsquo ; t like anyone else&amp;rsquo ; s children except my own . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Through my dad , I learned to value education . He always greatly respected teachers and taught me that teaching is very hard work indeed . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Granda Kopytko , Ottawa &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;What if a single victim had been American ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;Re : &lt;a href= " https : **33;78546;TOOLONG ... crash : Eight of those who perished were Ottawans returning home&lt;/a&gt; , Jan. 9. &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Where would we be now if one American had been on flight 752 ? &amp;nbsp ; It is my deepest hope that the tragic death of 176 people will somehow lead down a path to peace and stability in the Middle East . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Trevor Shaw , Ottawa &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;McCain was right to speak out on crash&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;Re : &lt;a href= " https : **33;78581;TOOLONG ... " data-portal-copyright= " AlexRaths " **28;79819;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1538131 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Rideau Street will miss this store&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A grocery store on Rideau Street that has been a solid contributor to downtown Ottawa life for decades ( now operated by Metro ) is closing next week . The move will make room for more residents in the neighbourhood , who will have less choice as to where to purchase their groceries in the immediate future . &amp;nbsp ; Longstanding and devoted employees are losing their jobs . A simple thank you for the years of good service we have received there does not seem quite enough . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ottawa City Hall continues to be bent on urban intensification through condo proliferation to increase the municipal tax base . This is often at the cost of the services that are needed to make neighbourhoods liveable and promote a community atmosphere that helps keep crime rates down and ensure Ottawa&amp;rsquo ; s downtown does not continue to deteriorate . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The closing of the Metro on Rideau to allow for the building of another high-rise condo and hotel complex is a perfect example of Ottawa&amp;rsquo ; s urban planning . While this will be a boon for the Loblaws on Rideau , it still boggles the mind of customers who , like me , shop at both establishments . When the Rideau Street area had fewer permanent residents than before the condo onslaught , residents of the area had much more choice of markets and services within walking distance of their homes . Our city planners apparently see no immediate problem in this state of affairs . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Competition between grocery stores can help keep prices lower for the older generations on a fixed income who have lived and paid taxes in the area for years , and maintain reasonably priced delivery services for people with disabilities and/or without cars , for the students from the University of Ottawa who regularly shop there with price-calculators in hand , and for Francophones like me who like to go where the language is still spoken in the city . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Some of the same planners and officials may oversee the LeBreton Flats re-development . But I walk Ottawa &amp;nbsp ; streets without concern or fear for my wellbeing and safety . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Gun violence aside , Ottawa is a very safe city , always has been and will be long into the future . It is incumbent on individuals and organizations with important interests and constituencies to dispel through public awareness and education the myth &amp;ndash ; fuelled by political expediency , emotion and media hype &amp;ndash ; that Ottawa is becoming a more dangerous place to live . Growing fears just do not match the facts . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But unfortunately , fear is also a fact . In Ottawa today , the fear of crime has far greater potential to destroy neighbourhoods than crime itself . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Those residents who are afraid to walk on Ottawa&amp;rsquo ; s streets are &amp;ldquo ; prisoners&amp;rdquo ; in the truest sense of the word . They will end up creating the very thing they fear : abandoned , dangerous streets . This must never be allowed to happen . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The mayor , police chief and other community leaders must present and interpret the facts as they are , namely , that residents who live in all parts of the city&amp;nbsp ; &amp;nbsp ; live in a very safe and civilized place . " data-portal-copyright= " PRAKASH SINGH " **28;80681;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1535357 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Military experience should be mandatory for our leaders&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There seems to be something wrong when the president of the United States , a man who managed to keep out of the military draft with a heel spur , is sitting in his private golf course deciding the fate of American soldiers in Iraq , while our own prime minister is making similar considerations from his Caribbean holiday retreat . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; As a suggestion , a spell in the armed forces might be a prerequisite for young men and women with federal political aspirations . The experience would at least give some perspective to those making life and death decisions for our soldiers . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Charles Morton , &amp;nbsp ; Manotick &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;We 're skating on thin ice over the climate&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;Re : &lt;a href= " https : **33;80711;TOOLONG ... weather keeps canal from opening&lt;/a&gt; , Jan. 4. &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Kelly Egan&amp;rsquo ; s article reminds me of my teenage years . It was the spring of 1968 when my family came to Canada and we moved into a house on Echo Drive right across from the Rideau Canal . We were fascinated by the beautiful and colourful tulips planted on flower beds along the canal . In the winter that year , we saw some teenage kids on the canal shovelling snow to make a skating rink . We thought , what an idea . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; So , my two younger brothers and I decided to pick up shovels and make one too . We created a 15 ft by 20 ft. skating rink in the canal and that was where we learned how to skate for the first time in our lives . We did this every winter after that until 1971 , when the NCC decided to build what became the world&amp;rsquo ; s largest and second-longest skating rink . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Today , it is sad to see that because of climate change the skating season is starting later and later and the season is shorter and shorter . It is unthinkable that one day there is a real possibility that skating on the canal will be no more . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Dono Bandoro , Ottawa &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Bus shelters do n't cause accidents&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt; &lt;/strong&gt; So , one year after the tragic Westboro bus crash , OC Transpo management has finally decided to review the structure and potential risk of its bus shelters in&amp;nbsp ; the case of accident . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; While this certainly needs to be examined ( and adjusted if necessary ) these shelters have never , to-date , posed any kind of measurable risk . A much more significant factor pertaining to potential crashes is OC Transpo&amp;rsquo ; s protocol and procedures for the selection and training of its drivers . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Despite a prior record of multiple crashes , the clearly inexperienced driver in the Westboro crash was assigned to a packed double-decker bus at the peak of rush hour . OC Transpo management , however , has never been challenged for its blatant lack of oversight in assessing the suitability and appropriate assignment of the driver . &amp;nbsp ; We can only hope that management is not continuing to assign packed rush-hour routes to similarly inexperienced drivers , despite whatever changes are eventually recommended for the bus shelters . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Bill Williams , Richmond &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Obesity is n't always about **26;80746;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;Re : &lt;a href= " https : **33;80774;TOOLONG ... blame the obese for climate change&lt;/a&gt; , Jan. 6. &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Thank you for publishing this article . " data-portal-copyright= " MEHDI MARIZAD " **28;81030;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1534662 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Moral principles should apply **25;81060;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;Re : &lt;a href= " https : **33;81087;TOOLONG ... calls for ' restraint ' in Iran following death of military chief Soleimani , &lt;/a&gt; Jan. 3. &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Will the killing of Qassem Soleimani lead to increased tensions with Iran ? Of course . Will this lead to cycles of retribution and more deaths ? Very likely . But sometimes assassination is a lesser evil ; would we have been better off with Adolf Hitler or Pol Pot eliminated before they rose to power ? Certainly . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The problem is , I am not sure how bad a person Soleimani was . Was he responsible for all the deaths suggested by the American government ? Was he planning further attacks , putting westerners at risk ? How can we separate propaganda from reality ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In the absence of reliable information , I believe that we have to impartially consider the morality of this action by the U.S. Immanuel Kant&amp;rsquo ; s categorical imperative states : &amp;ldquo ; Act only according to that maxim whereby you can , at the same time , will that it should become a universal law . &amp;rdquo ; In other words , act only in a way that would be acceptable if everyone acted in the same manner . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Many of us considered the invasion of Iraq in 2003 to be immoral and wrong . Would it have been acceptable for a Baathist to assassinate George W. Bush , or Donald Rumsfeld , or Tony Blair ? The U.S. military has used drones to attack citizens of Pakistan , Syria , Somalia and Yemen in recent years , with whom we are not at war . What would be our response if Bush or Barack Obama were murdered in retaliation ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I work hard to teach my young daughter to be a moral person and abide by such philosophies . I wish these same principles would be considered by our leaders . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Steve Kravcik , Manotick &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;U.S. is a country to be avoided&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; CTV News listed several countries not to visit after the assassination of Qassem Soleimani by the American government . " data-portal-copyright= " Getty Images " **28;81666;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1358295 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;On booze , a quick-fix mentality pervades society&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;Re : &lt;a href= " https : **33;81696;TOOLONG ... and our brains , Dec. 31&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;The article mentions that in the U.S. , groups are pushing to have cancer-warning labels pasted on beer , wine and liquor products . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;b&gt;&lt;/b&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;Alcohol warning labels epitomize a can't-hurt , why-not approach to public policy which is irresponsible and illogical . Good laws are driven by objectivity ( evidenced-based ) and hard facts -- not flawed public policy , political expediency , and emotion . Proponents selectively point to " evidence , " but ignore the big picture . The simple truth is that there is no compelling evidence to show that warning labels will have any effect on reducing the irresponsible use of beverage alcohol products . Several major research studies , including one by the World Health Organization , have concluded that labels have had no significant impact on consumption patterns . A WHO study in 2003 study found that labels affected awareness and knowledge , but had no direct impact on consumption or alcohol-related problems . Among youth , the warnings did not increase perceptions of alcohol risk , and may have even made the products more attractive to both drinkers and non-drinkers. &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;A quick-fix mentality pervades society . It affects personal expectations , business practices , and public policy . Unfortunately , quick fixes often sacrifice long-term progress for short-term actions , without doing anything to address the underlying causes . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;Emile Therien , &lt;/span&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;Ottawa &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;b&gt;Giving thanks to a longstanding delivery worker&lt;/b&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;Perhaps some of the Citizen 's long successful history can be attributed to those hired for the home delivery of the newspaper . As an early riser , and thanks to Citizen carrier Jane Piche , I have always appreciated seeing the Citizen at my door to read with a morning coffee . Piche , who has delivered the Citizen for many years , advised that with her 70th birthday approaching , she has opted to retire after delivering her final Citizen on Jan. 11 . Thank you , Jane Piche . Enjoy your well-deserved retirement . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;F.D . Boire , &lt;/span&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;Ottawa&lt;/span &g; ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;b&gt;Justice system failed bus driver in Humboldt crash&lt;/b&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;Re : &lt;a href= " https : **33;81731;TOOLONG ... Donham : The driver who killed the Humboldt Broncos received too harsh a **29;81766;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;Parker Donham made my day with his excellent column about the problem with victim-impact statements . I have always been against them and the case of Jaskirat Sidhu is a perfect example of why they have no place in a courtroom . We all feel tremendous empathy for the victims , but that emotion should not play a role in the sentencing . The type and degree of misbehaviour should . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;Sidhu had not been drinking or taking drugs , and was not speeding or texting . An accumulation of problems caused him to make a mistake which caused a tremendous tragedy . Had the bus been one minute later , nothing would have happened . What does society gain by locking him up for eight years ? The world has enough tragedies as it is . We do not need to add to them by potentially ruining an already devastated man 's life . Since the Canadian law , however , requires that the magnitude of the consequences be taken into consideration , the law must be changed . A conditional sentence of a few years would have shown the justice system to be working for society as a whole rather than responding to some of the parents ' desire for revenge . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;div id= " **39;81797;TOOLONG ... " &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;Here in Canada , our George Stroumboulopoulos railed against it . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;As a musician who has spent countless hours honing his ear , I hope its blaring light might soon fade , because it has ruined a decade of pop for me. &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;Alas , in 2020 and beyond , " auto-tune " will likely continue to erode and degrade not only the quality of pop music but also the critical ears of the listening public . Digital pitch " correction " erases an important expressive element in vocal and instrumental performance and has made dozens and dozens of weak pop singers , seemingly unable to accurately sing a simple scale or chord , into millionaires . Our beloved Glenn Gould and the electronic artist Wendy Carlos are known to have tuned their pianos and keyboards differently to achieve a more beautiful , more expressive effect . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;But needing a digital device to " just " pull off the two notes of " baby , baby , baby " is a sad joke in my book . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;The good news is that learning to sing in tune is not rocket science and there are programs and professionals that can really help . The bad news is that it is far from as simple as flicking a switch and turning a dial . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;Thomas Brawn , Orl ? ans&lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;b&gt;Cancelling the wind farm is the wrong move&lt;/b&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;Re : &lt;a href= " https : **33;81981;TOOLONG ... cancels nearly-built $200M wind farm over threat to bat populations , Dec. 10&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;To revoke a permit for the Nation Rise Wind Project near Crysler is a huge mistake . The project is to fill 100 mw of unused capacity on the local high transmission between Cornwall and Ottawa . Each windmill would power 3,500 homes . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;I have no stake in the project , except that like all residents of the township , I would benefit from cleaner electricity , more municipal tax revenue and grant money from the company to the municipality . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;There is no significant downside to building windmills in the centres of 29 cornfields in Eastern Ontario . This is private investment on private land . This is compliant with all laws and consistent with conservative philosophy . To revoke it 11 months after approval by the minister and with the project nearly finished is an error which will kill investment incentive in all of Ontario . Ironically , this is exactly the same error which led to the defeat of Kathleen Wynne in the Oakville gas plant fiasco . The electricity , if not produced here , will be produced somewhere else by fossil fuel or nuclear . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;The minister should not position himself on the wrong side of this issue and should reinstate the permit . This issue is very close to my heart . My husband received a cadaveric liver transplant in 1998 , and I donated a kidney to a friend in 2014. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Spain is often cited as an example of a successful opt-out system . It consistently has the highest number of donors in the world . In 2018 , Spain reached an incredible 48 deceased donors per million people . Canada , with an opt-in system , lagged well behind with only 20.6 deceased donors last year . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; However , these numbers don&amp;rsquo ; t tell the whole story . A recent study comparing systems in 35 countries , including Spain and Canada , concluded that there is &amp;ldquo ; no significant difference in deceased donation or solid organ transplantation activity between opt-out versus opt-in countries . &amp;rdquo ; Furthermore , there is &amp;ldquo ; no significant gain for established opt-in countries considering a switch to opt-out . &amp;rdquo ; So why does Spain have so many organ donors ? According to a review published in the American Journal of Transplantation , &amp;ldquo ; the Spanish success derives from a specific organizational approach to ensure the systematic identification of opportunities for organ donation . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Canada has had its own successes from organizational approaches . In 2008 , Canadian Blood Services was mandated to work with provincial and territorial partners to develop an integrated national system to improve organ and tissue donation and transplantation in Canada . Since that time : &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ndash ; The deceased organ donor rate has increased by 42 per cent &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ndash ; The number of transplant procedures has increased by 33 per cent &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ndash ; The number of donors after brain death has increased by 21 per cent &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ndash ; The number of donors after cardiac death has increased by 429 per cent &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; An opt-out system might help increase donation rates in Canada , if only because it raises awareness . But this data suggests we can do far more by continuing to support the work of Canadian Blood Services and their network of partners . And , of course , speak to your loved ones . Regardless of the consent system used , your family will be consulted . Make sure they know what your wishes are . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Caroline Vanneste , Ottawa &lt;a href= " https : **25;82729;TOOLONG : //blo ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Tasers are n't a bad thing&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;Re : &lt;a href= " https : **33;82756;TOOLONG ... at odd with community policing , Dec. 24&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;The letter writer seems to think that the police 's use of Tasers affects their sense of community policing and that it would be much better to physically handle the person in order to disable them . " data-portal-copyright= " Errol McGihon " **28;82909;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1529824 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Spot the nickel 's hidden message&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;Re : &lt;a href= " https : **33;82939;TOOLONG ... can you spare a nickel ? A tale from the world of coin collecting&lt;/a&gt; , Dec. 23&lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Your article on the history of our nickel missed one interesting item of trivia . The wartime victory nickel had a morse code message imprinted around the inside of the rim . It can be clearly seen in the front page photo of the 1943 nickel . The message reads : &amp;ldquo ; We win when we work willingly . &amp;rdquo ; In 2005 , the Mint issued a commemorative victory nickel but the morse code message was missing . The reason the Mint gave for this omission was that the original message was not bilingual . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; John Hendy , Ottawa &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Full nickel set almost complete&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; For some reason , I have been collecting nickels for decades ( I actually prefer stamps ) . I have 1921 to 2019 complete , except for seven nickels . In 1926 , two styles of nickel were minted : some with the &amp;ldquo ; 6&amp;rdquo ; beside the &amp;ldquo ; 2&amp;rdquo ; as per normal ; and for some unknown reason , some with the &amp;ldquo ; 6&amp;rdquo ; quite a bit further from the &amp;ldquo ; 2. &amp;rdquo ; I have only seen a picture of the two types side by side . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I agree with the gentleman in the article saying 2019 was hard to find and after a couple of trips to the bank to purchase rolls of nickels , he finally got one . Nearly the end of 2019 and I have yet to find one . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Thanks for the fun ! &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Lynda Haddon , Perth &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; **25;82974;TOOLONG our World War coins&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It was interesting to see the 1943 Canadian Victory Nickel on the front page of the Citizen . The following two references may be of interest : the Victory Nickel is a Canadian coin produced between 1943 and 1945 . The design of the coin was intended to promote the Canadian war effort during the First World War . The coin was dreamed up by Thomas Shingles , master engraver of the Royal Canadian Mint . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A similar coin was released in 2005 to mark the 60th anniversary of the end of the Second World War . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Canadian Victory Nickel was also known as the World War II Propaganda Coin . This original Victory Nickel was first struck in 1943 during the Second World War by the Canadians as a piece of pure propaganda . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A flaming torch and &amp;ldquo ; V&amp;rdquo ; for Victory ( Churchill&amp;rsquo ; s famous salute ) dominates the design . &lt;span style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt;The coin was in fact the only coin in the Commonwealth whose design reflected the fact that the nation was at war . Used by service men and women , as well as those at home , it reminded people of the war effort . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Doug Hughes , Kemptville &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;A big New Year 's resolution for Canadians&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Although New Year&amp;rsquo ; s resolutions are often personal goals , here&amp;rsquo ; s one I feel many Canadians may embrace : Fix 24 Sussex Drive . The official residence for Canada&amp;rsquo ; s prime minister has been in a state of disrepair for many years and essentially vacant since November 2015 . Built in 1868 , the residence needs major renovations . Spending millions of dollars to maintain a deteriorating building and not deciding what to do is less than responsible stewardship . Whether the building is repaired or replaced , it is time to set political considerations aside and make a decision to get the project moving toward resolution . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; F. The term &amp;ldquo ; SITREP&amp;rdquo ; is essentially a military term and is a form of status reporting that provides chain of command decision-makers and readers with a quick understanding of the current situation . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It offers a clear , concise understanding of the situation , focusing on meaning or context , in addition to the facts . It does not assume the reader can infer what is important or not and what actions or decisions are needed . It in fact calls out what needs to be done . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The &amp;ldquo ; reports&amp;rdquo ; provided in this news piece on light rail are preludes to speaking points and not &amp;ldquo ; SITREPS. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Eric R. Stephen , Ottawa &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;A taxi driver full of kindness&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Late last week , after a hectic early morning dealing with family matters , I left my mother&amp;rsquo ; s retirement home on Beechwood Avenue and was pleased to find my Blueline cabbie patiently waiting for me . The driver , George , and I had a great ride . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He was from a family of 10 children who immigrated to Canada from Lebanon in the 1980s , whereas I was from a family of four kids who immigrated from Ireland to the Ottawa Valley in the 1830s. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; We had much in common including our view of the importance of caring for and learning from the elderly , putting family and community first , and gratitude for living in a country like Canada . When I reached into my purse to pay for the ride my wallet was nowhere to be found ( it had fallen out in my husband&amp;rsquo ; s car ) . Not only was George not upset about this , he actually wanted to give me money to help get me through the day . What a kind soul . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Thank you , George , for making my Christmas merrier. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Judith Gorman , Ottawa &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Trudeau 's exit strategy is brilliant&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;Re : Ascent of Freeland beginning to look like Trudeau 's exit , Dec. 21. &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Andrew MacDougall has brilliantly summarized our prime minister&amp;rsquo ; s exit strategy . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; What better way to exit than this ?
